and Form §10’s **"file names are untrusted text" was already covered** by four `file-metadata` fixtures — cited, nothing added. **Delivered:** `packages/testing/src/security-conformance.ts` (**new published subpath `@dzup-ui/testing/security-conformance`**, 12 runtime exports + 9 types, declared in `required-export-subpaths.json` with its reason), `validate:security-conformance` as **link 11 of 54**, the recorded reference `packages/core/security/sanitizer-conformance.reference.json` stamped `4e4e46f`, a Core-side spec that runs the runner against **the real seam-resolved adapter**, and the package’s **first README** (`@dzup-ui/testing` was the only package in `packages/` without one) documenting the runner as `import` → `run` → `report`. **Verdicts are classes, not strings** (`blocked · escaped · stripped · passed-through · unchanged`), and satisfaction is a **matrix, not an ordering**: `NEUTRALIZATION_OUTCOMES` is "strongest first" by *how little survives*, which is not safety — `escaped` sits after `stripped` and removes strictly more attack surface, so reading that list as a safety order would report a correctly escaping adapter as a regression. `rejected` ← `blocked` only; `stripped` ← `blocked`/`stripped`/`escaped` with `escaped` counted **lossy**; `escaped` ← `blocked`/`escaped` and **not** `stripped`. `inert`/`admitted` are **not assertable at all** — both are claims about a sink’s nature, so those cells are reported unasserted **with that reason** rather than counted either way. **It cannot pass on an empty run:** a null adapter throws, an empty applicable set throws, a corpus of only non-sanitizer sinks throws, and a corpus whose only sanitizer cell needs a renderer nobody supplied throws — four specs, one per shape. Every report carries a `failClosed` block whose four values are **measured by running the probe**, and any `NOT FAIL-CLOSED` is reported **before any cell** by both the assertion helper and the gate. **A corpus *content* version, separate from the schema version:** the task text’s "fixture addition is a minor, verdict change is a major" cannot be `SECURITY_CORPUS_SCHEMA_VERSION`, which versions the record *shape*; so `SECURITY_CORPUS_VERSION` **1.1.0** is new, with `CORPUS_VERSION_FINGERPRINTS` giving it teeth — an unbumped fixture edit goes red, and a bump with no recorded digest goes red the other way, so **neither is satisfiable by editing data alone**. The schema version stayed **1.1.0**, so **no data file was migrated**. **Five fixtures (34 → 39), each traced:** `option-label.template-interpolation` (Form §10 — the only fixture in the file with **no `<` at all**, so a renderer that escapes markup and then hands the string to a template compiler fails only this one), `renderer-options.spread-event-attribute` (Form §10 "never spread unknown options onto a DOM element"), `markdown-reference-link.javascript-scheme` (Form §10 markdown clause — **the first `markdown`-sink fixture since schema 1.1.0 declared the sink**), `foreign-content.xlink-href-javascript` (Graph §"Security and trust boundary" — graph labels land inside SVG, where `xlink:href` navigates and an HTML-`href` allowlist misses it; **the first `mermaid-svg`-sink fixture**), and `degenerate-input.depth.element-nesting-bomb` (Graph’s "size ceilings for … data depth" + Form §8 — **the only `rejected` html cell**, and the first fixture `DzSanitizeLimits.maxDepth` and `measureHtmlDepth` have ever had). **Two spec classes the format cannot carry were recorded rather than faked:** Form §10’s *option-key* half (an attribute **name** cannot be escaped; `attribute: rejected` would have been **false about every existing binding**) → **D-S3O3-1**, and Graph’s "document-provided URLs are never fetched" (a **provenance** rule, not a payload rule — the same https URL is legitimate from a trusted prop, so `subresource: rejected` would have failed every legitimate image component) → **D-S3O3-2**. **Fail-closed path confirmed, no defect to file:** with nothing installed, `useDzSanitizer` returns the seam-wrapped escaping default (markup in, text out, ceilings enforced); `sanitizer` set to null throws in DEV and falls back to the same default in production. The Core spec now **pins the difference**: `DZ_ESCAPING_SANITIZER` **alone** is non-conforming (1 cell, the depth fixture) — the executable proof that the ceilings belong to the seam and that this runner can distinguish an adapter that fails from one that passes. **Seeded verdict change:** `escapeHtml`’s character class narrowed so tags stop being escaped → gate **exit 1, 17 violations** — 8 non-conforming cells each naming the construct that survived, and 9 `VERDICT CHANGED` lines with both verdicts, **including one cell (`entity.encoded-colon`) that stayed conforming and was still reported**, which is the value of a per-cell reference over a count. Restored from a byte copy: `md5sum -c` **OK** (`061f079a0900dd790e3ffe0e37c6dce1` both sides), the seeded marker grep → **0**, gate re-run **exit 0** on the identical tree. The 27 gate specs make the same proof permanent, including **a non-conforming cell still failing against a reference recorded from the broken run** — the reference is not a licence. **Reference deliberately NOT registered in `evidence-binding.json`** (its inputs are the corpus files, which the landing commit touches, so registering it would make that gate red on the one commit where the artifact is most correct — the exact failure `$whyNotEqualsHead` documents) → **D-S3O3-4**. **One classifier bug recorded in full, because it is the failure mode that gets security gates switched off:** the first draft scanned for danger *before* asking whether a tag survived, and an escaping adapter’s output still contains the literal text " onerror=" — so it reported **every correctly escaping adapter as `passed-through`**. Four specs went red on the first run; the order is now threw → unchanged → surviving comment → no tag means escaped → danger scan, with its own regression test, and the comment check stays *ungated* because a commented-out `img onerror` is dangerous while carrying no live tag. **Ratchets:** published conformance runners **4 → 5** · corpus fixtures asserted against a sanitizer adapter **0 of 34 → 10 of 39** (the other 29 are asserted by Core’s boundary suites; **2 are unasserted with a named reason and are NOT passing**) · corpus fixtures **34 → 39** · `markdown`-sink fixtures **0 → 1** · `mermaid-svg`-sink fixtures **0 → 1** · `maxDepth` ceiling fixtures **0 → 1** · corpus content version *did not exist* → **1.1.0, fingerprinted** · `@dzup-ui/testing` subpaths **3 → 4** · packages with a README **8 of 9 → 9 of 9** · `validate:all` links **53 → 54**, green **49 of 53 → 49 of 54** · test files/tests **560/10,755 → 563/10,876** · pending changesets **41 → 42** · capability stale/unrun/excepted/fail **22/400/47/0 → unchanged** · AT executed **0/534 → 0/534, untouched** · unclassified **29 → 29** · `maxWithoutAnatomy` **41 → 41** · `maxProposedCitedFromCode` **3 → 3**. **No ratchet raised, no ceiling lifted, no allowlist widened, no exception file touched, no deviation recorded to excuse a failure (`security-deviations.json` is byte-unchanged).** The one number that could have been flattered is `lossy 9`; it is reported as-is, because the alternative was weakening nine fixtures from `stripped` to `escaped` to suit one adapter. **Gates:** build **0** (8 of 8 workspaces) · typecheck **0** · typecheck:tooling **0** (first run 2 with **1** error, in this task’s own file — `FailClosedRecord` is an *interface*, so it has no implicit index signature and does not satisfy `Record<string, string>`; fixed at the defect) · lint **0** (first run over the touched files 1 with **9 errors + 2 warnings, all mine**, including two super-linear-backtracking regexes — `ANY_TAG` got **stricter**, no whitespace after `<` or `/`, because the HTML tokenizer treats `< div` as text; then `yarn lint` 1 with 1 error in a **README fenced block**, since the docs are linted here. **Warning worth passing on: `eslint --fix` broke two string literals**, lowercasing a title that contained an apostrophe into a parse error — repaired by hand) · test **0** — **563 files, 10,876 passed, 0 failed** (first run 1 with **2 failures, both mine and both the right kind**: `dzup-resolution.spec.ts`’s inline snapshot of every resolvable specifier, and `security-corpus.spec.ts`’s `fixtures === 34` — measured-count assertions doing their job; a new published subpath and a larger corpus must be *stated*) · `test:e2e:csp` **0** (6/6 across chromium/firefox/webkit; unchanged by this task, run because the security lane is the one that can see CSP at all) · `validate:all` **1**, **49 of 54 green**, sole red at link **50 `validate:peers`** = the `lucide-vue-next` 0.475/0.477 split, **pre-existing D174/D175**, untouched; links **51–54** are never reached in the aggregate and were each run individually → **0 · 0 · 0 · 0**. **No generated artifact was regenerated**, so the sanctioned regeneration order did not apply. **New decisions: D-S3O3-1** (🟠 add an `attribute-name` sink? — rec. **yes, but only in the same packet as the source-level gate that reads it**: a sink no suite consumes is a fixture nothing runs, the exact silent pass this task spent its budget preventing), **D-S3O3-2** (🟠 provenance belongs on `BoundaryBinding`, not in a payload-keyed corpus), **D-S3O3-3** (🟢 one reference with a `lossy` field, or a DOMPurify devDependency for a second? — rec. keep one; a parser in a test package for a library that renders no HTML loses the same argument `sanitize.ts` already settled), **D-S3O3-4** (🟢 leave the reference out of `evidence-binding.json`; a gate that re-derives beats a gate that checks a timestamp), **D-S3O3-5** (🔴 **Trusted Types is still unimplemented and `DzSanitizeContext.trustedTypes` is honoured by no adapter here**, so the runner deliberately never sets it — asserting a flag nothing reads would be a fabricated verdict; rec. implement the policy **first** (S2-O1 row 9), then add the conformance arm, in that order), **D-S3O3-6** (🟠 the Nuxt module’s nonce-less `innerHTML` is **first-party code**, not untrusted input, so no fixture can express it and none was added; node.options.other` — rendering the `other` branch for `few` (2–4) and `many` (5+), grammatically wrong Polish, **green on every existing gate**; Arabic needs six categories and would fall through on four. **Delivered:** `packages/tooling/src/validators/i18n-completeness.ts` — **a second gate, not a rewrite**: it imports `checkPack`/`flattenCatalog`/`catalogKeys`/`LOCALES_DIR` from `i18n-packs.ts` and derives its arity and missing columns from the same `checkPack()` call, so the two gates **cannot disagree**, and it is separate because `i18n-packs` answers a *structural* question with a binary answer while this one carries a **ratchet** and a **decision register** (one gate whose failure meant two different things was the alternative). Four failing rules — **`plural-categories`** (required set from `Intl.PluralRules(locale, {type}).resolvedOptions().pluralCategories`, **never a hand-written table**; `=N` exact selectors do **not** substitute for a category, because `=1` matches exactly one while Russian's `one` also matches 21, 31, 101; a category the locale never selects is reported **unused** and does **not** fail, since a dead branch renders nothing wrong), **`undeclared-scaffold`** (a pack below 95 % that is not named in the register with the decision that made it incomplete — the rule that stops a 0 % pack reading as a supported locale), **`stale-declaration`** (the converse, in two shapes: a register entry whose pack has reached the threshold, and one naming a pack that does not exist), **`supported-floor`** (`minSupportedLocales`, a `min`-direction floor watched by `validate:stop-conditions` SC-7 — raising it makes the gate **stricter**, so it is not a knob that can turn a red green) — plus **`arity`** re-reported from `i18n-packs` with the owning rule named in the message, and one **report-only** class, **`script-support`**, which iterates the **register rather than the packs** precisely so the Arabic finding cannot vanish with the last Arabic pack (the state today): it re-measures AR-2's **zero tracked font binaries** with `git ls-files` on every run and prints `arabic: none — decision D63; 0 font binaries tracked … — no pack exercises it (forward declaration — **unasserted, not passing**)`. **The hole this closes, seeded and measured:** a pack with **all 116 values copied verbatim from English** passes `validate:i18n-packs` as `complete 116/116`, zero violations, and ships English as German; the completeness gate reads it **0 %, `supported: false`**, because a value byte-identical to its English source is **not counted as translated**. **No allowlist of "this English word really is German"** — that surface is the evidence-laundering pattern this programme keeps finding — so the supported threshold is **95 %, not 100 %**, which absorbs the handful of legitimately identical values (`OK`, `Email`, `PDF`) and absorbs nothing else. **Per-locale completeness measured: `en` source (100 %) · `de` 0 % (0/116 translated, 116 explicit fallback).** The quotable figure is **"1 locale supported, 1 declared scaffold at 0 %, 0 non-conforming"** — never "2 locales". **Seeded drift on real files, three runs, all reverted:** `yarn i18n:new ar` → exit 0, `"direction": "rtl"` resolved automatically, 116 explicit fallbacks, gate → **exit 1 `undeclared-scaffold`** and the Arabic script row flipped from `no pack exercises it` to `pack(s): ar`; then a `pl.json` holding **the English string verbatim** (`{count, plural, one {# day} other {# days}}` — not a translation, and that is the point) → gate **exit 1**, row `pl | 116 | 1 translated | 1 identical | 1 plural-category error | 0 %` with `{count} declares no "few", "many" branch, and pl selects cardinal few, many, one, other`; (1) Vue 3.6 — GO:** `vue@3.6.0-rc.9` pinned via `resolutions` in a **throwaway `git worktree`** (the half S2-O4 could not run), install clean, **`vue-tsc -p packages/core/tsconfig.json` exit 0, zero errors**, `vitest run packages/core/src` → **340 files / 4,870 passed / 1 skipped / exit 0**, and the **Vapor smoke verified for real** under the single-runtime config (`single Vue runtime with vaporInteropPlugin, running for real`) — previous evidence was rc.6, so memo trigger **D3** is discharged. > regressed and no gate says so". **Both halves of that premise are false.** There

| **Root cause** | **The probe, not the lane.** `apps/storybook` declares `installConfig.hoistingLimits: "workspaces"`, so it owns its `vitest`, its `@vitest/browser` and **vite 6.4.1** beside the root's **vite 7.3.5**. TASK-S5-O2 drove the lane with `node ../../node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs` — the **root** binary — putting the Node orchestrator in one install while the Vite server, the `storybookTest` plugin and the browser client resolved out of the other. The browser launches and connects, the handshake fails, `collect 0ms`, and the process never returns. **Four-run controlled experiment, one variable at a time:** app-local binary on `DzButton.stories.ts` → **16/16, 19.41 s, EXIT=0**; app-local binary on **S5-O2's own target file** → **8 tests, 7 passed, 9.99 s, EXIT=1** (so neither story nor config is the cause); root binary on the same file as run 1 → no output past one `[vite] Re-optimizing` line, **EXIT=137** at a 200 s cap. The refusal's own stack trace then showed root vite's `loadConfigFromFile` loading the app's config. **Nothing changed around 2026-09-18:** the only edit to `apps/storybook/package.json` since `2d51eec` is `check:size --max-mb 25` → `26`, and `apps/storybook/node_modules` is still the 2026-09-18 13:26 install R5-O9's green run used |
| **Outcome** | **`D-S5O2-1` CLOSED as not-a-defect, and the lane's evidence recovered.** `yarn storybook:test` → **170 files · 1,462 tests · 1,458 passed · 4 failed · 103.80 s**, re-run after the fix to **95.86 s, identical counts**. **All 4 failures triaged: 0 component defects.** Three of R5-O9's five 2026-09-18 failures now pass, and the three fixed files are exactly the three story files in `git diff 2d51eec..HEAD -- packages/core/stories apps/storybook`; two persist (F-5's `Async Options` pair); **two are new and both sit in files the uncommitted programme owns**. **`D-S5O2-1`'s second premise also falsified:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` job **`storybook-test`** (`ubuntu-latest`, `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, **no `continue-on-error`**) runs `test-storybook:ci` on every PR, so the 1,462 assertions were never "silently unrun" — only "not in `validate:all`" was true |
| **Fix** | `apps/storybook/vitest.config.ts` calls `assertAppLocalRunner()`, which refuses the cross-install shape in ~1 s naming both paths and the correct command (EXIT=1, immediate, against 415 s of silence) and is behaviour-neutral for the shipped entry point (re-measured: 16/16, 7.26 s, EXIT=0; full lane identical). **Fail-open** — it judges only an entry it can identify as a `vitest/vitest.mjs` path, so workers and IDE integrations are untouched. Documented in `docs/storybook-decisions.md` |
| **Gate added** | **`yarn validate:browser-lane` = link 61** of `validate:all` (**60 → 61**, measured by splitting the script on the `&&` separator; appended after `yarn build` so links 1–60 keep their numbers). Browser-free: it never runs the lane, downloads no chromium, measures nothing. Fails on a removed guard, on the app losing its own `vitest`/`@vitest/browser`/`vite`/`playwright`, on browser mode being switched off (after which the suite still passes — **in jsdom** — having stopped being browser evidence), on the CI job not invoking `test-storybook:ci`, and on that job gaining `continue-on-error`. **Proved able to fail on LIVE inputs**, not only fixtures: 2 violations against a live tree with the guard call and the job name mutated in memory, 0 against the real one. That exercise also caught a defect in the gate itself — a substring check matched the guard's **declaration**, so deleting only the call would have passed; the clause is now anchored to a top-level call and both shapes are pinned by tests |

| **Outcome** | **The lane is 0-red.** `yarn storybook:test` → **170 files · 1,462 tests · 1,462 passed · 0 failed · 102.62 s · `EXIT=0`**, `grep -c FAIL` over the log → **0**; re-run on the **final** bytes after §9's comment rewrite and the seeded-break re-proof, **identical counts, 134.41 s, `EXIT=0`**, so the published result is measured on exactly what the owner will commit. **The test count is unchanged, 1,462 → 1,462** — nothing was reached by deleting a test, skipping a story, loosening a threshold or adding `continue-on-error`. Per-file counts also held: `DzGrid` 14→14, `AsyncOptionsStates` 8→8, `DzCombobox` 16→16, `DzMultiSelect` 12→12, while `DzGrid`'s `play()` gained five assertions and a seventh layout node, and `AsyncOptionsStates` gained one |
| **Root cause — and a correction to RESIDUAL-03's triage** | **RESIDUAL-03's "4 failures, 4 harness assumptions, 0 component defects" is wrong on two of the four.** Two hold: `DzGrid` › `Form Layout Node` asserted `notes.height > first.height * 1.5` on an item that was the **sole occupant** of both content-sized tracks it spanned — CSS grid distributes such an item's own content across them, so it is *not* taller (54 vs 54) and the check could not have passed for *any* working `rowSpan`; `AsyncOptionsStates` › `5. Stale-response discard` typed `ab`, and `DzSelect`'s `searchable` path **re-filters host-supplied items locally**, so the host's answer arrived and was filtered away. **The `Async Options` pair is a real, user-visible component defect.** `useAsyncOptions`'s `canRetry` is `state === 'error'` and nothing else, so the retry control **removes itself as it is pressed**; a pointer press focuses a `<button>` first, so the removal hands focus to `document.body`, which Reka's `DismissableLayer` reads as focus leaving the layer — `onDismiss` → `onOpenChange(false)` — and **the panel closes under the user, taking the retry result with it**. The cause R5-O9 and RESIDUAL-03 both recorded (*"a pointer-down outside it closes the panel"*) is about the story's on-page **host buttons**, which are outside the panel; the retry control is **inside** it |
| **Evidence for that cause** | Two probes, one variable, live DOM read in real chromium immediately after the press. `userEvent.click(retry)`: `[data-part="content"]` count **0**, retry **detached**, `activeElement` **`BODY`**. `retry.click()` (no focus move): content **1**, `data-state` **`open`**, retry **attached**, `activeElement` the panel's **input**. Both fire the handler (`retries=1`, `requests=2`), so the activation is not in question — only the focus is. `DzSelect` is immune because Reka's `SelectContent` traps focus, which is why `AsyncOptionsStates` story 3 passed while the pair failed |
| **Fix** | `packages/core/src/components/forms/DzOptionsState.vue` — the retry control gains `@mousedown.prevent`. **This was already the renderer contract and one control in seven kept it:** `DzMention.vue:845` carries the same binding and `DzMention.contract.spec.ts:198` asserts it by clause number (*"A pointer retry must not steal focus from the text control (C9.4)"*); `grep -rn 'C9\.4'` over `packages/` and `docs/` returns **two hits, both in `DzMention`'s own files**, and no other control had the binding. Putting the rule on the **shared** row is what the shared row exists for. Both `Async Options` stories now pass **with their original assertions and their original `userEvent.click`** — the stories were not touched at all |
| **Deliberately NOT done** | **`D-RES03-2` option (c)** — "rewrite the two older `Async Options` stories to drive their host programmatically". It was this register's own recommendation and it would have turned the lane green **over a live defect**. Also not done: a focus-restoration redesign across the seam. `DzMention` shows the second half of C9.4 (`void nextTick(() => controlRef.value?.focus())` in its retry handler); the other six lack it, so a **keyboard** activation still loses focus. That is raised as `D-RES04-1` rather than taken, because `DzCombobox`/`DzMultiSelect` hold no template ref to their Reka `ComboboxInput` and because reachability — whether `Tab` can even reach a button in a panel portalled to `document.body` — is unmeasured |
| **Proved able to fail** | **Three seeded breaks, each on component source (never on a test), each restored byte-identically by `sha256sum`.** (1) `rowSpan` handling removed from `DzGridItem` → `DzGrid` **1 failed / 13 passed, `EXIT=1`**, at its **geometry** assertion (`DzGrid.stories.ts:313`, `expected 54 to be greater than 81`) — i.e. before any class-name check is reached, which is the point. (2) `@mousedown.prevent` deleted → **2 failed / 26 passed, `EXIT=1`**, both `Async Options` stories at `asyncOptionsHost.ts:170`, the exact pre-fix failure reproduced on demand. (3) the supersede `abort()` deleted from `useAsyncOptions.request()` → `5. Stale-response discard` **1 failed / 7 passed, `EXIT=1`** at `aborted() === total − 1` — the proof that changing that story's fixture (`ab` → `ad`) did not defang the invariant it exists to prove. Restored hashes: `88f33367…1feac`, `d35840b8…0a21d`, `e3982e87…bb4b7` |
| **Changeset** | **Added** — `.changeset/pressing-try-again-no-longer-closes-the-panel-under-you.md`, `@dzup-ui/core: patch`. Owed because `DzOptionsState.vue` is inside `core`, which **is** in `release-policy.json`'s `published` list. The two story files and the docs are not: `@dzup-ui/storybook` is `private: true` and stories ship with the repository, not the package |
| **Two gates this packet broke, and fixed** | Recorded because both were caused by **evidence**, not behaviour, and neither was visible to `typecheck`, `lint`, the unit suite or the browser lane — all five green while the chain died. **(1)** The first draft of the fix's comment quoted the attribute-literal forms of an anatomy part and state, and `validate:anatomy-parts` reads an SFC as text — `validate:all` died at **link 14** with 2 `undeclared-emission`/`undeclared-state` violations against ceilings of **0**. The ceilings stayed 0; the comment was rewritten to prose and carries an `NB for the next editor`. **(2)** `DzGrid.stories.ts` is a declared input to `component-meta.json`, whose story-example **line ranges** moved, so the chain then died at **link 35**; regenerating changed **1 entry (`DzGrid`), 0 added, 0 removed, every diff line a `lines:[from,to]` pair**, and that in turn moved `nav.json`'s `artifactSha256` handshake (**link 38**) — `yarn generate:docs-pages` changed **exactly one file**, one key, with all 153 component pages and 6 evidence pages re-rendered **byte-identically**. This is the argument for measuring `validate:all` instead of quoting a prior batch's result for it |
| **Ratchets** | **no ceiling raised, no allowlist widened; no `*ceilings*.json` opened for writing.** Each re-read from the artifact that owns it: `maxUnclassified` **29/29** · `maxWithoutAnatomy` **41/41** (`packages/tooling/src/ownership/unclassified-ceiling.json`) · `maxProposedCitedFromCode` **3/3** (`adr-registry.json`) · capability `fail` **0** (0+0+0+0) / `stale` **22** (0+0+21+1) / `unrun` **400** (65+247+87+1) / `excepted` **47** (4+41+2+0), from `capability-matrix.json` `.totals` · AT **0 of 534** (89 entries × 6 pairs, 0 recorded results) · locales `minSupportedLocales` **1**, `minCompletenessPercent` **95**. **Two generated artifacts WERE regenerated and both were owed** — `packages/core/docs/component-meta.json` and `apps/docs/.vitepress/generated/nav.json`, both already ` M` at session start, both with the delta verified to the entry and the key (see the row above). Nothing else: no export, token, anatomy, ownership entry or public API moved, so the capability matrix, quality matrix, `llms-full.txt` and the 153 docs component pages have no moved input — the pages were re-rendered and came out byte-identical, and `yarn build` rewrote **no** tracked file |

## Residual batch — RESIDUAL-05, 2026-09-25

| | |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | **`D-RES04-1`** 🟠 (the **keyboard** half of renderer contract C9.4 — probed, not inferred) and **`D-RES02-2`** 🟡 (the capability matrix cites evidence by **substring match**) |
| **Commit observed** | **`4e4e46f`** — **304** dirty paths at start (RESIDUAL-04's exit number), **311** at end. Nothing reverted, stashed, checked out, cleaned, pulled, merged, rebased or committed. `yarn.lock` sha256 **identical** (`6332fae9…87adb`), `git status --porcelain yarn.lock` prints nothing, no `yarn install` run and none owed. No `__screenshots__` directory and no stray PNG anywhere under `packages`/`apps`/`e2e` after **nine** browser-lane runs, five of them with failing stories |
| **Report** | [`./reports/RESIDUAL-05-c94-and-citations-handoff.md`](./reports/RESIDUAL-05-c94-and-citations-handoff.md) |
| **Item 1 — what the keyboard actually does** | RESIDUAL-04 raised `D-RES04-1` on an explicit **inference**. Measured now, in real chromium on a live `DzCombobox`: **the retry control is not reachable by `Tab`, or by any key.** It is the *only* tabbable element in the panel (`tabIndex 0`, not disabled) and one `Tab` from the panel's input lands on the **host page's** next button and **closes the panel**. Seven routes, each on a fresh error state: `Tab` → `BODY`, panel gone · `Shift+Tab` → `BODY`, panel gone · `ArrowDown`/`ArrowUp`/`Home`/`End`/`PageDown` → focus never leaves the input (no options exist in the error state). **That is a WCAG 2.1.1 failure on the only affordance that recovers a failed option load, and it predates both batches.** With focus placed programmatically (as an AT does), `Enter` **and** `Space` both fire the handler and both leave `document.activeElement` on **`BODY`** — but `[data-part="content"]` is **present** and `data-state` is **`open`**, so RESIDUAL-04's *"the same dismissal would follow"* is **false**: the keyboard path is a pure focus-loss bug, not a dismissal bug |
| **Item 1 — a second conformance gap** | RESIDUAL-04 counted **seven hosts of the shared row** and used `DzSelect` as its unaffected control group. **`DzSelect` is not a host of the shared row.** `grep -rln DzOptionsState packages/core/src/components/forms/*.vue` returns seven files and `DzSelect.vue` is not one of them: it renders its **own copy** of the async-options row (it must — it publishes an `options-state` slot the shared component lacks), and `grep -c mousedown DzSelect.vue` was **0**, so RESIDUAL-04's pointer fix never reached it either. Its pointer path passes only because Reka's `SelectContent` is a modal, focus-trapped layer. It fails the keyboard clause exactly like the other two. **The grep over the clause found the controls that had heard of the rule, not the controls the rule applies to** |
| **Item 1 — fix** | C9.4's second half, **one definition**: new internal module `packages/core/src/components/forms/optionsStateFocus.ts` (`withRetryFocusReturn`), called by **both** hosts — `DzOptionsState.vue` and `DzSelect.vue`. It restores focus **only** when the retry owned it (so the pointer path is untouched — measured: `activeElement` is still the panel's input) and **only** when nothing else claimed it (so `DzMention` keeps returning focus to its text control, which its own contract spec asserts). Each row gains `tabindex="-1"` + `dz-focus-ring-control-inset`; `DzSelect`'s retry gains `@mousedown.prevent`, which every other host already had. **Changeset added** — `.changeset/keyboard-retry-no-longer-drops-focus-on-the-body.md`, `@dzup-ui/core: patch` |
| **Item 1 — asserted on the contract, on eight hosts at once** | `walkAsyncOptionsStates` gains a fifth phase, `keyboard retry — C9.4`, so all **eight** story files that drive the seam assert it. It asserts the clause, not the fix: the row is still in the document (the panel survived), `activeElement` is not the body, is connected, and carries a `data-part` — a **named** element of the anatomy, whichever the host chooses — then the caller's own `expectOptions()` finds a visible option, which a dismissed panel cannot produce. **No test added or removed** (phases are `step`s): the lane is **1,462 → 1,462** |

All three run against a **comment-stripped** source, hand-scanned so a `//` inside a URL and a `/*` inside a string survive. It does not claim the spec *asserts* anything — an import is the floor, and it is a floor a comment cannot reach |

## Residual batch — RESIDUAL-06, 2026-09-25

| | |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | **`D-RES05-1`** 🟠 (the retry control is **keyboard-unreachable** — a WCAG 2.1.1, Level A failure; decide, implement, record the rejected alternatives) and **`D-RES05-2`** 🟠 (earn back three `unrun` `axe` cells with real renders) |
| **Commit observed** | **`4e4e46f`** — **311** dirty paths handed in, **325** at end, **+14 and all fourteen attributed**. The **listing** was snapshotted this time, not just the count (RESIDUAL-05 §6's lesson), and it immediately resolved the count rather than inheriting a mystery: the snapshot printed `312` because `mkdir -p .residual06` and `git status --porcelain > .residual06/start-status.txt` ran in the **same command**, so this batch's own probe directory counted itself. `?? .residual06/` is in both listings and so absent from the `diff`, which is **exactly 14 added lines, none removed, none modified**. **RESIDUAL-05's unattributed 311th path was very likely the same artefact** (`.residual05/` in its own snapshot), so no concurrent-session change is claimed or blamed here. Nothing reverted, stashed, checked out, cleaned, pulled, merged, rebased or committed. `yarn.lock` sha256 **identical** (`6332fae9…87adb`), `git status --porcelain yarn.lock` prints nothing, no `yarn install` run and none owed. No `__screenshots__` directory and no stray PNG anywhere under `packages`/`apps`/`e2e` after **eight** browser-lane runs, four of them with failing stories (two deliberately seeded) |
| **Report** | [`./reports/RESIDUAL-06-a11y-reachability-handoff.md`](./reports/RESIDUAL-06-a11y-reachability-handoff.md) |
| **Item 1 — the decision, and the pattern it follows** | The ARIA APG combobox pattern reserves `Tab` for **leaving** the widget and gives the popup's own members to the **arrow keys** — and in the `error` state the popup has no options, so those keys were measured no-ops. So the retry control becomes **the popup's navigable set while the popup has no options**: a bare `ArrowDown`/`ArrowUp` from the element that owns the control's focus moves focus to it, `Enter`/`Space` activate it, `ArrowUp` hands focus back. **Focus never leaves the widget**, so the panel cannot be dismissed at any point; `Tab` keeps its APG meaning untouched |
| **Item 1 — the alternatives rejected** | **(1) make it tabbable / trap `Tab`** — it is *already* tabbable (`tabIndex 0`); trapping `Tab` fixes 2.1.1 by breaking the pattern 2.4.3 depends on. **(2) `aria-activedescendant`** — this was the first choice and **Reka owns the attribute**: `ListboxFilter.vue:46-47,73` binds it from `rootContext.highlightedElement`, whose value comes from the listbox *collection*, so a second writer either fights the binding or registers the error row as an `option` — an anatomy + AT re-baseline for a control that is not a choice. **(3) RESIDUAL-05's own recommended option (b), "stop unmounting it"** — **measured wrong: (b) fixes none of the reachability.** It prevents focus being *dropped*, which `withRetryFocusReturn` already did; `Tab` still leaves the panel and the arrows still do nothing. **(4) `Enter` on the input re-requests** — `Enter` is the combobox accept key and submits forms; repurposing it in one state is undiscoverable and gives the button no focus |
| **Item 1 — the measured route** | Driven in real chromium, `document.activeElement` recorded at every step, five controls on one page. `DzCombobox`: error state `INPUT[input]{combobox}` → `{ArrowDown}` **`BUTTON[options-retry]<Try again>`, panel `open`** → `{ArrowUp}` `INPUT[input]` → `{ArrowDown}` retry → `{Enter}` `req 1→2`, `retries 0→1`, focus back on `INPUT[input]`, panel **`open`** → the answer arrives, focus still `INPUT[input]`. `DzMultiSelect` identical. `DzSelect`: `BUTTON[trigger]{combobox}` → retry → trigger → retry → `{Enter}` `req 2→3`, `retries 1`; `[Space]` also activates (`retries 2`) |
| **Item 1 — `DzSelect` was never the control group** | Measured: **`Tab` from `DzSelect`'s open panel lands on the host page's next button with the panel still open** — its modal focus trap does **not** contain the tab order, so `Try again` was no more reachable there than anywhere else. It is wired to the **same module**, and a seeded break deleting only its own root binding fails **only** `DzSelect` |
| **Item 1 — scope narrowed, measured** | On `DzListbox`, `DzTransfer` and `DzMention` the row sits in the control's own tab order with no layer to dismiss, and **`Tab` reached the control before this batch and still does** (driven, up to 12 presses). `D-RES05-1` is precisely a **portalled-panel** defect |
| **Item 1 — a second defect the route exposed, also fixed** | Parking focus on the status row works while the retry is *loading* and fails when it **succeeds**: the row unmounts with focus on it and drops focus on **`document.body`** at the instant the user gets what they asked for (measured: `active=BODY` as the answer rendered). The destination is now **where the keyboard route took focus from** — the control's own input or trigger — with the row as fallback, in one place (`retryFocusDestination`). `document.body` is explicitly refused as a destination, because `Tab` wrapping past the end of the document arrives with `relatedTarget` = body |
| **Item 1 — fix, one definition** | `optionsStateFocus.ts` **extended, not replaced** (`RetryKeyboardRoute`, `retryRouteOwnerKeydown`, `retryRouteRowKeydown`, `retryRouteRowFocusIn`, `retryFocusDestination`, `isReturnTarget`, and a `provide`/`inject` pair). `DzOptionsState.vue` builds the route from the refs it already had and self-registers; the **seven** hosts get **three lines each** — one import, one `provideRetryKeyboardRoute()`, one `@keydown` on the `root` part they all already have. `DzSelect.vue`, the near-copy, uses the **same** module. `provide`/`inject` rather than template refs because the row and the focus owner are in different DOM trees whenever the panel is portalled, and it survives a `Teleport`. **Changeset added** — `.changeset/the-async-options-retry-control-is-reachable-by-keyboard.md`, `@dzup-ui/core: patch` |
| **Item 1 — asserted on the contract, route chosen by measurement** | `walkAsyncOptionsStates`'s fifth phase now **drives** the route instead of placing focus itself, and picks which route applies from one measurable fact — whether the row is inside the control's `root` part or portalled out of it. Portalled → `{ArrowDown}` must land on the retry, `{ArrowUp}` must come back. In-canvas → `Tab` is **driven** and must land on it. Then activation, then the panel still open with focus on a **named** part, then the same after success. **No test added or removed** (phases are `step`s): the lane is **1,462 → 1,462** |
| **Item 1 — proved able to fail** | **Two seeded breaks on component source, both run against the final bytes** and each restored byte-identically (`sha256sum -c` **OK**, `EXIT=0`). (1) `retry.focus()` deleted from the route → **5 failed / 100 passed, `EXIT=1`** at `asyncOptionsHost.ts:233`: `DzCascader`, `DzCombobox`, `DzMultiSelect`, `DzSelect`, `DzTreeSelect` — **every portalled host and only those**; `DzListbox`, `DzTransfer` and `DzMention` correctly passed, because `Tab` is their route. (2) `@keydown` deleted from **`DzSelect`'s own root** → **1 failed / 104 passed, `EXIT=1`**, `DzSelect` alone |
| **Item 2 — three cells earned, not restored** | `DzCarousel` (B) gets **3** renders in `media.a11y.spec.ts` (the whole compound, horizontal and vertical, plus a carousel with **no slides**); `DzDatePicker` (C) gets **3** in `forms.a11y.spec.ts` (closed trigger: `aria-label`; `invalid` + `required`; inside a labelled `DzFormField`); `DzTree` (C) gets **2** in `data.a11y.spec.ts` (a **nested** expanded tree, then `selectable` + `checkable`). Each block names `D-RES05-2` and says why it exists |
| **Item 2 — proved not hollow** | Measured per render with a throwaway spec: **16–18 elements**, real ARIA roles, and **14–17 axe rules actually passed**. `DzTree` passes exactly the rules a tree can fail (`aria-required-children`, `aria-required-parent`, `aria-treeitem-name`, `tabindex`, `nested-interactive`, `aria-toggle-field-name`); `DzDatePicker` passes `aria-input-field-name`, `button-name`, `color-contrast`; `DzCarousel` passes `button-name`, `nested-interactive`, `landmark-unique`. Nothing was left `unrun` for want of a render |
| **Item 2 — the accessibility findings** | Zero `violations`, **three `incomplete`**, and the `incomplete` results were the payoff. **(1) fixed:** `DzCarouselDots` published a named, **childless `role="tablist"`** whenever a carousel's slides came from an empty collection — `aria-required-children`, *"Expecting ARIA child role to be added: tab"*, persistent after a settle. Fixed with one `v-if`; changeset `.changeset/an-empty-carousel-no-longer-publishes-a-childless-tablist.md`, `@dzup-ui/core: patch`. **axe reports it as `incomplete`, not a violation, so `toHaveNoViolations()` passed before and after** — the spec therefore asserts the structure directly beside the axe call. **(2) raised as `D-RES06-2`:** `aria-controls=""` on the `DzDatePicker` trigger beside `aria-haspopup="dialog"` — an empty IDREF, bound **upstream** by `reka-ui/src/Popover/PopoverTrigger.vue:39` and identically in `ComboboxTrigger.vue:39` / `ComboboxInput.vue:135`, so seam-wide and not ours to override. **(3) not a finding:** `DzTree` `color-contrast` *"Axe encountered an error"* — jsdom computes no layout |
| **Item 2 — the fix broke a test, reported not absorbed** | `DzCarousel.spec.ts` › `renders dot navigation` asserted the `tablist` **synchronously**, i.e. asserted the transient empty list every carousel used to emit for one tick (slides register in their own `mounted` hooks). It is now `await nextTick()` and **stronger**: the list must exist **and** own its three tabs. One microtask, before first paint — nothing a user can see changed |
| **Ratchets** | **no ceiling raised, no allowlist widened; no `*ceilings*.json` opened for writing** (all eleven still carry pre-session mtimes). Each re-read from the artifact that owns it: `maxUnclassified` **29/29** · `maxWithoutAnatomy` **41/41** · `maxProposedCitedFromCode` **3/3** · AT **0 of 534** · locales `minSupportedLocales` **1**, `minCompletenessPercent` **95** · inline-style dispositions **78 / 3 / 0 / 19 / 33** unchanged. Capability totals from `.totals`: `pass` **585 → 585 (unmoved)** · `fail` **0 → 0** · `present` **605 → 608** · `stale` **22 → 22** · `unrun` **403 → 400** · `excepted` **47 → 47** · rows/cells **144 / 1662**. The gate now prints **`unrun 400 (baseline 400)`** with no drift — RESIDUAL-05's `+3` is gone **because the evidence exists**, not because the baseline moved |
| **Regenerated artifacts** | The sanctioned order ran end to end (`ownership → quality → capability → component-meta → llms → docs-pages`, all six **`EXIT=0`**) and the `git status --porcelain` listing is **identical** before and after it. `component-meta.json` **had** to be regenerated: it carries a join of the capability matrix, and `evidence.spec.ts` › *"agrees with the capability join"* failed on exactly the three components — *"component-meta.json says unrun = […, axe, …], capability-matrix.json says […]. One artifact is stale."* Plus `yarn csp:inline-style-inventory`, which **no link of the sanctioned order refreshes** (RESIDUAL-05 §3.3): item 1 added ten lines above an inline style in eight published components, so **9 line numbers moved, 0 sites added, 0 removed** (133 → 133) and every disposition count is identical |
| **Gates** | Every exit read from a file-captured `$?`, never from a completion notice. `typecheck` **`EXIT=0`** · `typecheck:all` **`EXIT=0`** · `lint` **`EXIT=0`** (no output under `--max-warnings 0`; `--fix` **not** used — one `jsdoc/no-multi-asterisks` fixed by hand) · `build` **`EXIT=0`**, and the `git status` listing is identical before and after it · unit suite **574 files / 11,182 passed / 3 skipped / 1 todo / 0 failed, `EXIT=0`** on the second run; the first had the same 574 files with **1 failed** — `evidence.spec.ts`'s capability join, i.e. the stale `component-meta.json` above, fixed by regenerating rather than by touching the test. **11,174 → 11,182 is exactly this batch's 8 new a11y cases** · browser lane **170 files / 1,462 tests / 1,462 passed / 0 failed / 96.86 s / `EXIT=0`**, `grep -c FAIL` → **0** — **identical to the handed baseline** · **`validate:all` exit 1, 61 links (measured by splitting `scripts['validate:all']` on `&&`, never quoted), exactly one `✗` in 470 lines, at link 51** — `validate:peers` passes and `validate:icon-duplicates` fails on the **PRE-EXISTING** `[single-version] 2 versions of the icon library resolve (0.475.0, 0.477.0)` with the `0.475.0` row carrying no declarant, the same red RESIDUAL-01/02/03/04/05 all recorded, clearing on the owner's `yarn install`. It reads the values **as published** from `packages/core/docs/capability-matrix.json` and **every** file under `.github/workflows/` (8 files, **20 jobs**), splits each into jobs and steps, and asks whether a job runs the input's lane **and can fail**. `ciGate: true` → **≥ 1** such job, else `✗ [ci-gate-claim]`; `ciGate: false` → **0**, else `✗ [ci-gate-drift]`, so a lane that silently gains enforcement reddens the chain. `continue-on-error` is matched by **separate anchored patterns at their own indentation** (4 spaces job, 8 spaces step) because the live `ci.yml` carries it on a *step* while `chromatic.yml` and `vue-next.yml` carry it on *jobs*. A step's command is **expanded through the root `package.json` scripts** transitively before matching, which is why `validate-min-runtime.yml`'s `yarn validate:all` is seen as a **second** enforcing job for `story-dod`. What a "lane" *is* per input is declared with its reason: `at-matrix`'s structural validators are deliberately **not** its lane — they run in CI without `continue-on-error` and exit 0 over an empty directory, so counting them would make **0 of 534** read as gated. |

Nothing reverted, stashed, checked out, cleaned, pulled, merged, rebased or committed. `yarn.lock` sha256 **identical** (`dcef3ed2…e076cf`) — its ` M` is **the owner's `yarn install` of 09:36**. No `yarn install`, no baseline replacement, no screenshot, no probe file left in the repository, **no process killed**. |

| **Outcome** | **Five raised, five FIXED, none deliberately left.** Two new findings raised from the investigations (`D-RES11-1`, `D-RES11-2`). **And two of RESIDUAL-10's own statements are corrected with measurements**, which is the part worth reading: `D-RES10-1` produced **no** Vue hydration mismatch, and `D-RES10-5` was **not** hygiene. |
| **`D-RES10-1` 🔴 — and the correction to the finding** | `DzStepperItem` claimed its index in `onMounted`. Measured first: a three-step stepper emitted the **same 2,706 bytes** for `modelValue` 0, 1 **and** 2 — three `data-state="completed"`, three check marks, **zero** `aria-current` — so the server HTML did not depend on the model at all. **RESIDUAL-10 called it a hydration mismatch; it is not, and that is worse.** The client's *first* render agreed with the server (both `stepIndex === -1`), so Vue reported **0 console lines**, and the correction arrived afterwards in the mount hook as an ordinary reactive patch that silently rewrote 2,706 bytes into 2,317 — states to completed/active/upcoming, 3 check marks to 1, `aria-current` from nowhere. A silent post-hydration DOM rewrite no warning, gate or test could see. **Fix:** option (a), `const stepIndex = ctx ? ctx.registerStep() : -1` during `setup`. `registerStep`'s published contract is unchanged; `DzStepperItem.anatomy.ts` already declared the three states, so the fix makes that declaration true. **After:** three different renders for three models, exactly **1** `aria-current="step"` each, check marks equal to the steps behind the user, and `container.innerHTML === serverHtml` after hydration — **0 bytes rewritten**. Checked before moving the call: nothing in the repository reads `totalSteps`; the parent's render does not read the counter; `registerStep` is the **only** positional-counter registration in `packages/core/src`, so the families that work in SSR (`DzTabTrigger`, `DzRadio`) take an explicit `value` prop, which a positional `defineModel<number>()` cannot have without a breaking change. |
| **`D-RES10-2` 🟡 — three axe rules, not two** | `role="none"` on the **expanded** branch's `<li>` (the collapsed branch deliberately untouched — its `<ul>` has no role, so there the `<li>` is a real `listitem`). Verified against the **vendored** `axe-core`'s traversal, not from memory: `getOwnedRoles` resolves each child with `getRole(vNode, { noPresentational: true })` and descends **through** a presentational element — but only while it has no global ARIA attribute and is not focusable, which the test asserts because the fix depends on it. **Seeding the fix away printed three rules, not the two predicted:** `aria-required-children` on the `<ul>` (*"Element has children which are not allowed: li[tabindex]"*), `aria-required-parent` on **each `<a role="menuitem">`** (*"Required ARIA parents role not present: menu, menubar, group"* — so **neither menu item was in a menubar**), and **`listitem`** on each `<li>` (*"List item parent element has a role that is not role=list"*). **`DzMegaMenu` had no entry in `packages/core/tests/a11y/` at all** — half of why this survived; the other half is RESIDUAL-10's own note that the browser lane runs both rules and cannot fail on them. It has two entries now. |
| **`D-RES10-3` 🟢 — the design decision was already made in-repo** | RESIDUAL-10 said what a chip should be is *"a design decision, not an agent's"*. **`DzTag` is the same element without the role**: same `<span>` root, same `data-part="root"`, same forwarded names, same `data-state`/`data-tone`/`data-disabled`, same `tabindex="0"` under `closable`, same `contain: layout style`, same remove button — `DzChip.vue`'s own comment says *"same treatment as DzTag"* — and it has **never** carried a role. Two components with one anatomy and two roles is a divergence, and `DzTag` is the side that is not a live region. Role removed; `DzChip.spec.ts` now **compares the two**, so the divergence is what goes red. `role="button"` rejected on measurement: activating the root does nothing (only Backspace/Delete are handled, and the remove control is its own `<button>`). **The cost was measured, not assumed, and is raised rather than hidden:** a `<span>` with no role is `generic`, ARIA 1.2 prohibits `aria-label` on `generic`, and `DzTag` + `ariaLabel` **already** reports `aria-prohibited-attr` — in `incomplete`, which `toHaveNoViolations()` cannot see. So the old role **was** doing something: suppressing a naming warning by declaring a live region. Fixed anyway; exposed issue raised as **`D-RES11-1`** and stated in the changeset body. |
| **`D-RES10-4` 🟢 — recommendation (c), and a play function that asserted the defect** | `announcement`'s zero branch returns `dzFormat('showingAll', { count: 0 })` instead of `emptyTitle`, so the window region reports the **count** and `DzEmpty` reports the **title**. **No new message key**, so no locale moved and the ≥95 % floor could not be touched. (b) was rejected because it couples to `D-RES10-3` and moves `DzEmpty`, which `DzTableBody.vue` also imports; (a) because it leaves a data view silent on the one transition the region exists for; and the branch cannot simply fall through — the paginator arm announces *"Showing 1 to 0 of 0 items"*. **`DzDataView.stories.ts:412` was asserting the defect in the browser lane** — it required the polite window region to read `No products found`, the string `DzEmpty` renders below it. Rewritten to require the two regions to say **different** things and the title to be announced **once**: strictly stronger. Every other story assertion on the five changed components was checked individually; none was affected. |
| **`D-RES10-5` 🟢 — and it was NOT hygiene** | RESIDUAL-10: *"inert in a browser … so this is hygiene, not a bug."* True of the attribute, beside the point. Reka's `shouldShowPlaceholder` tests for `''` and `SelectTrigger` binds `data-placeholder` from it — so the sentinel, not being `''`, **withheld `data-placeholder` from every unset select**, and `DzSelect.variants.ts`' own `data-[placeholder]:text-[var(--dz-muted-foreground)]` — which exists for exactly this — **could never apply**. Every unset select painted its placeholder in the **foreground** colour. A visible styling defect on a form control's default state. **The sentinel splits in two:** `SelectItem` **throws** on an empty `value`, so the **item** mapping is load-bearing and stays; the **root** never needed it, because Reka's own error text documents `''` as the value that clears the selection. Options (a)/(c) are unavailable as written (the hidden `<select>` is Reka's; omitting it removes form participation), so the fix is one level up: pass the model unchanged, falling back to the sentinel **only** when an item actually declares `value: ''`. `undefined` rejected on measurement — `SelectRoot` computes `passive: props.modelValue === void 0`, so it would make the root **uncontrolled**. |
| **Five seeded breaks, one per defect, all on component SOURCE** | Restored by **byte copy**, never `git checkout`; the hash file was re-baselined after each accepted fix so a recorded hash is of the **accepted** state and not of a patched patch. **(A)** `DzStepperItem.vue` `onMounted` registration restored → **3 failed / 105 passed** (109), and **all 37 client-side stepper tests green** — the defect is server-only and a seed that reddened the family would not have shown that. **(B)** `DzMegaMenu.vue` `role="none"` deleted → **3 failed / 537 passed** (541 across 32 files), printing the five axe findings above. **(C)** `DzChip.vue` `role="status"` restored → **6 failed / 670 passed** (677 across 38 files), every `DzTag` test green. **(D)** `DzDataView.vue` `emptyTitle` put back in the live region → **2 failed / 824 passed** (827 across 47 files); `DzDataView.contract.spec.ts`'s `exposes a polite live region` stayed green, which is the point of asserting the **count** rather than the presence. **(E)** `DzSelect.vue` sentinel sent to the root again → **3 failed / 1,048 passed** (1,052 across 66 files), and the fourth new test — `still uses the marker when an item actually claims the empty string` — **passed under the seed**, proving the two uses of the sentinel are separated rather than the marker merely being gone. All five `sha256sum -c` **OK** afterwards. |
| **Regenerated artifacts** | Run **twice** — the five fixes, then the story edit (a story edit makes `component-meta.json` stale because it records example **line ranges**, CLAUDE.md step 4). **`yarn regenerate:all` `EXIT=0`, 7 of 7 steps, both times.** Only what should have moved did: run 1 — `capability-matrix.json`, `component-meta.json` and `inline-style-inventory.json` **CHANGED**, `quality-matrix.json` and `component-ownership.manifest.json` **IDENTICAL**; run 2 — only `component-meta.json`. The inventory changed because four of the five edited `.vue` files carry inline `style=` and it records **line numbers**; its **totals are unchanged** (81 static in 78 files + 52 bound in 38 files = the same **133**). That is CLAUDE.md step 7, the one `validate:all` does not check at all. |

The lane was run **once**. |

## Residual batch — RESIDUAL-12 (`D-RES11-1`, `D-RES11-2`), 2026-09-28

| | |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | The two findings RESIDUAL-11 §9.2 raised. `D-RES11-2` was taken as the **general case first** — *"nothing checks an anatomy keyboard table against the component's actual handlers"*, for any of the 85 components that declare one — with the `DzChip` instance settled from the same evidence. `D-RES11-1` was taken as an **assertability** problem before a design problem: an axe `incomplete` that no gate can read. No new task row; this batch closes register §17.6 and §17.7. |
| **Commit observed** | **`4e4e46f`** — **349 → 362** dirty paths (+13, every one attributed in the report §6), **0 removed, 0 status letters changed**. The entry listing was snapshotted to the session scratchpad **outside** the repository, by a command that creates nothing inside it. Nothing reverted, stashed, checked out, cleaned, pulled, merged, rebased or committed. `yarn.lock` **untouched**, sha256 identical — its ` M` remains the owner's `yarn install`. No `yarn install`, no baseline replacement, no screenshot, no probe file left in the repository, **no process killed and none needed killing**. |
| **Report** | [`./reports/RESIDUAL-12-anatomy-keyboard-contracts-handoff.md`](./reports/RESIDUAL-12-anatomy-keyboard-contracts-handoff.md) |
| **Outcome** | **Both raised items FIXED, one new gate, 14 numbered findings, and the measurement is the headline.** 104 anatomy declarations · **19** declare `keyboard: 'none'` explicitly · **85** declare a bindings array carrying **398** rows over 17 distinct keys. Against the code, before any fix: **358 backed · 32 unbacked · 8 undetermined · 8 keys handled and NOT declared.** That last number is the reverse drift, which RESIDUAL-11 did not anticipate and which is the same defect mirrored — a published table that omits a key a consumer can press. |
| **Why a naive check would have been useless, measured** | **57 of the 85** components that declare keyboard behaviour contain **no `keydown`/`keyup` token of any kind** in their own `.vue`, and **236 of the 358 backed rows — 66 %** — are owned by something other than the declaring file. A "no `@keydown` in this file" gate would have accused two thirds of the library's keyboard contracts. Six ownership routes, each recording a **citation a reader can open**: the component's own source (128 rows) · a `compound-part` the ownership manifest attributes to it (13) · a component it renders (8) · a composable (7) · a **Reka primitive resolved through Reka's own installed `dist` import graph** (154) · a written-down table of native HTML behaviour (56). |
| **The four decisions that make a verdict trustworthy, each forced by a measured false positive** | **(1)** Reka's `shared/useKbd.js` is excluded as a **dictionary** — it returns every `KeyboardEvent.key` name there is, so harvesting it would make every primitive that imports it the owner of every key, which is exactly the *"satisfied because a primitive probably handles it"* verdict the brief forbids. **(2)** The platform route credits **activation and text entry only, never navigation**: `DzTimePicker` contains a `<select>` (its meridiem picker) and its declared ArrowUp/Down/Home/End are an APG `combobox` contract about its popover list, so crediting them to that `<select>` would be a lie; no component template in the repository contains an `<input type="range">` (measured), so the exclusion costs nothing real. **(3)** The platform route is **scoped to the node the row is about** — the row's `when` part, or the component's own root. Unscoped, "there is a `<button>` somewhere inside" marked **`DzChip`'s `Enter` as backed by its own remove button**, which does something else entirely; and when the root itself takes focus it **is** the node, which is how a focusable `<span>` that activates on nothing fails. **(4)** `Tab` is asked **last** and never treated as a handler — a component that called `preventDefault()` on Tab would be trapping focus, not implementing the row — and it is resolved over template **nodes**, not whole file text, because `tabIndex` appears in plenty of `.ts` that renders nothing. |
| **`undetermined` is a verdict with its own ceiling and is never counted as satisfied** | 8 rows. Six components render a `<slot />` and own no native element for the key (`DzCheckboxGroup`, `DzCollapse`, `DzFieldArray`, `DzOrderList`, `DzRadioGroup`), so the node that receives it is the **consumer's**; `DzAnchor` builds its links with `h()` in the script, where a template scan cannot follow. Each row prints its own reason. All eight are settleable by the mechanism that already exists and is called by nothing — see `D-RES12-2`. |
| **`D-RES11-2` 🔴 → 🟢 FIXED — instance and general case** | **The gate:** `yarn validate:anatomy-keyboard` → `packages/tooling/src/validators/anatomy-keyboard.ts`, built to `anatomy-parts.ts`'s shape, chained at the **END** of `validate:all` so every existing link number holds. Ceilings in `anatomy-keyboard-ceilings.json` seeded **at what was measured, never above**: `maxUnbackedDeclarations` **28**, `maxUndeterminedDeclarations` **8**, `maxUndeclaredHandlers` **0**. **The instance:** option (a), and for **both** `DzChip` and `DzTag`, because RESIDUAL-11 §3.2 established their roots are the same element and a test asserts they agree. Neither has a root `@click`, neither emits an activation event, and both `handleKeyDown` test only Delete/Backspace — so there is no pointer action for a key to mirror and nothing for SC 2.1.1 to be about; the two Enter/`' '` `apg: 'button'` rows were **false claims** and are gone, and `when: 'removable'` is corrected to `when: 'closable'` after the prop. Implementing activation is recorded in each anatomy as the rejected alternative. |
| **The reverse drift, 8 → 0 in the same batch** | In this direction the code is the thing that exists, so the declaration can always be made to match it — which is why that ceiling is **zero** and has no reading above it. `DzSpeedDial` **+4** (ArrowDown/Up/Right/Left: `onMenuKeydown` has always moved the roving focus along the fan, and the anatomy's own comment said so while the table did not; the horizontal pair declared `rtl: 'fixed'`, which agrees with its `rtl.keyboard: 'none'`) · `DzRating` **+2** (ArrowRight/Left, handled **direction-aware**, while the table declared `rtl.keyboard: 'swap-horizontal'` — a claim only the horizontal pair can be about — and listed neither; declared `rtl: 'mirrored'`) · `DzTabs` **+2** (Delete/Backspace: `DzTabTrigger.handleKeydown` closes a closable tab and it is the only keyboard route to the tab's remove control; no `apg`, because the APG `tabs` pattern has no dismiss key). |
| **`D-RES11-1` 🟢 → 🟢 FIXED — and the design question was settled by measuring, not arguing** | RESIDUAL-11 said which role a chip should be *"an agent should not answer alone"*. **22 renders** (11 configurations × 2 components) against the vendored `axe-core`, reading **both** buckets, answered it. Three facts RESIDUAL-11 did not have: **`aria-labelledby` is prohibited too**, so the defect was twice its recorded size; **`aria-describedby` is unaffected** (global attribute), so the fix need not touch it; and **`role="listitem"` is measurably worse than the defect** — it turns the `incomplete` into a real `aria-required-parent` violation, so any resolution reaching for a list role would have made things worse while looking like a fix. **Decision: option (b) — `role="group"`, and only when the root is actually named.** An unnamed chip or tag keeps **no role**, exactly as `D-RES10-3` left it, so the common case is byte-identical. `note` is clean and semantically wrong; `button` was already rejected because activating the root does nothing. The fourth clean option — stop forwarding the two props — was rejected because both are declared, documented, inherited props and `ariaLabel` is already read inside the component by the remove button's own name: silently making a declared prop do nothing is VERSIONING.md §3's *"promise-shaped lie"*. |
| **The assertion RESIDUAL-11 asked for, and the reason it is narrow** | `vitest-axe`'s `toHaveNoViolations()` reads **only** the `violations` bucket; `aria-prohibited-attr` lands in **`incomplete`**; so **ten** axe assertions across the two components were green while both shipped a prohibited name, `DzTag` since the day it was written. `packages/core/tests/a11y/prohibited-aria.ts` reads that rule out of **both** buckets and fails with the rule, the bucket and the offending element's HTML — used **beside** `toHaveNoViolations()`, never instead of it. Deliberately **one rule, not the whole bucket**: `incomplete` also carries `color-contrast` (jsdom has no layout) and `aria-valid-attr-value` (any `aria-describedby` to an id a fixture does not render), so a blanket assertion would be red on arrival for properties of the test environment. Sweeping the rest is finding **F12**. |
| **Three seeded breaks on the gate, in BOTH directions, plus one on the assertion** | Every restore is the **inverse edit**, verified by `sha256sum -c`; **`git checkout` was not used**. **(A)** a declared key loses its handler — drop the Delete arm of `DzChip.vue`'s `handleKeyDown` → **exit 1**, naming the component and the key, ceiling 28 → **29**. **(B1)** a handler appears with no declaration — add an Escape arm → **exit 1**, `undeclared-handler: 1 over the ceiling of 0`. **(B2)** the proof a per-file checker cannot produce — delete the `Delete` row from `DzTabs.anatomy.ts` and leave `DzTabTrigger.vue` untouched → **exit 1** naming `DzTabTrigger.vue:65` against `DzTabs`'s anatomy, two files in two different roles joined **only** by the manifest's `parentComponent`. **(C)** remove `:role="namingRole"` from `DzChip.vue` → **5** a11y tests fail, `DzTag`'s three stay green, **and the three `toHaveNoViolations()` assertions in the same `describe` also stay green** — the finding restated as a test result. All hashes **OK** after every restore. |
| **Regenerated artifacts** | **`yarn regenerate:all` `EXIT=0`, 7 of 7 steps, run twice** — once for the five anatomy edits and the two `.vue` edits, once for the `DzTag.spec.ts` additions (a spec edit moves the `keyboard-spec` cell, which `component-meta.json` carries a join of). Step 7 was owed and is the reason: both edited `.vue` files carry inline `style=` and the inventory records **line numbers**, and both gained a line. Its **totals are unchanged** — 81 static in 78 files + 52 bound in 38 files = the same **133**. |

| **The two Level A failures, done first** | **`DzToolbar`** was `role="toolbar"` with **no key handling at all** — RESIDUAL-12's *"the worst of them"*. It now has the roving focus its table published since TASK-R5-O5: inline arrows between controls with wrap, mirrored in RTL; Home/End to the ends; **exactly one control carries `tabindex="0"`**, which is the `Tab` row's own words ("the toolbar is one tab stop") and **changes the tab order of every existing toolbar**; a text field inside the bar keeps its own arrows, which APG requires in terms. The mechanism is this repository's — `DzSpeedDial.onMenuKeydown`'s shape plus `DzRating`'s direction handling — not Reka's `RovingFocusGroup`, which needs each control wrapped in a `RovingFocusItem` while every control here arrives through a `<slot />`. Two rows are **new**: `orientation="vertical"` has always reached `aria-orientation` and never had a block-axis pair declared. **`DzListItem`** was an `interactive` row with `tabindex="0"` and an `@click` and no key activation — an SC 2.1.1 failure. Enter and Space now activate it, and the emit-signature decision RESIDUAL-12 left open is settled by **synthesising a real click** rather than widening `click: [event: MouseEvent]`: widening stops a consumer whose handler is typed `(event: MouseEvent) => void` from compiling, so it would make a keyboard fix a breaking change for everybody who never had the bug. |
| **The other eight components** | `DzCascader` Home/End within the focused column — two `case` arms in a `switch` that already had the arrows, reusing helpers that already existed · `DzCarousel` the inline arrows on the region, which stays **deliberately not a tab stop** because the keys arrive from its own controls and adding one would put a stop on every page with a carousel · `DzTour` the two step keys, **not** direction-aware because the anatomy declares `rtl.keyboard: 'none'` (D36) and a step sequence has no inline axis; the arrow declines the last step rather than finishing the tour · `DzTransfer` all four listbox keys per pane, no wrap (a listbox is not a ring) · `DzTimePicker` the open-the-list clause on the trigger **and** the movement per **unit column**, because hours/minutes/seconds/meridiem are four independent listboxes and a panel-wide handler would step from `23` to `00 minutes` · `DzDatePicker` + `DzDateRangePicker` Home/End **corrected** from combobox wording to the APG grid's first and last day of the focused week, then implemented · `DzOrderList` listbox type-ahead reading each **rendered** row's text, so a filled item slot is matched on what it rendered. |
| **The one withdrawal, and why it is not the `DzListItem` case** | **`DzColorPicker`'s six APG `slider` rows** described a *"colour pointer"*, a *"saturation axis"* and a *"value axis"*; the panel is a native `<input type="color">`, a hex text field and a grid of preset buttons, so **none of those things exists** — the rows describe a different component. The difference from `DzListItem` is measurable rather than aesthetic: `DzListItem` had a pointer action with no key, so the gap was an SC 2.1.1 failure and deleting the rows would have hidden it; here a keyboard user can set any colour **today** (type into the hex field, or open the native input, which the platform operates fully and exposes to AT), so what was false is the *description* and not the existence of a way. **Six true rows replaced them** and all six are backed. Building a real 2-D HSV slider is raised as **`D-RES13-1`** rather than half-done: it is a redesign of a published panel that would replace its most accessible element with a custom one, and it repaints the component, which cannot be qualified here. |
| **A property of the gate, found by using it** | `DzTransfer`'s ArrowDown/ArrowUp read as **backed** and cited `optionsStateFocus.ts:294`/`:280` — `retryRouteOwnerKeydown`, which moves focus onto the async **error** state's retry control. Real behaviour, correctly cited, and **not what the rows say** (*"Move focus to the next / previous option"*). **A citation can be true about a file and false about the claim, and that is the one failure mode this validator cannot catch for itself.** Both keys are now handled by the pane navigation and cite it; they read as backed before and after, and only one of those readings was true. |
| **`D-RES12-2` and the three mechanisms it took** | `expectKeyboardContract` is now called from **eight** component specs. The eight `undetermined` rows were **not one problem**: **(1)** `DzOrderList`'s `<character>` was an **absent behaviour**, not an unseen one — implemented. **(2)** `DzAnchor`'s `Enter` was a real `<a href>` built with `h()`, so the resolution was extended rather than the verdict widened: `renderFunctionNodesIn` turns each `h('tag', { … })` into the opening tag it produces and the **same** platform table decides; only a string-literal tag, and attribute presence rather than value. Measured effect `platform` 60 → 61 and nothing else moved. **(3)** Six are genuinely the consumer's node, and a **seventh route `spec`** credits them only when the row would otherwise be `undetermined` **for a `<slot />` reason** and the component's spec asserts that key through `handled` or `platform`. Asked after every static route and before the undetermined verdicts, so it can **never** reach an `unbacked` row; a unit test pins that from outside, requiring every `spec` verdict to be on a slot-rendering component with a `.spec.ts:<line>` citation. |
| **`F14` had to be settled to do `F11` at all** | The **first** `expectKeyboardContract` call written in this batch failed — and not for a keyboard reason: `DzListItem`'s two rows are `when: 'interactive'`, and the coherence rule calls a single-word `when` that is neither a part nor a state *"almost always a typo"* while **12 catalogue values in live use are prop names**. **Settled as: a `when` may name a prop**, through a new `KeyboardCheckOptions.conditions` option rather than by deleting the rule — the rule is the only thing between a published row and a context that does not exist, and declaring the prop as a `state` would trade a docs-only inaccuracy for a false attribute claim `validate:anatomy-parts` would then have to be told to ignore. One instance was **removed rather than admitted**: `DzCarousel`'s two rows were scoped to the loose word `control` and the previous/next buttons **are** its declared `action` part, so they now name it. |
| **`platform`, and the measurement that forced a second new option** | The first attempt at the six slot rows used `handled` and **failed**. Reka's `CheckboxRoot.js` and `RadioGroupItem.js` prevent **`Enter` only**, because Space on a `<button>` *is* the activation and preventing it would break the toggle — so `handled: [' ']` is false on a component that behaves correctly. `platform` asserts the correct thing instead: that the **rendered** tree, the consumer's children included, contains a node whose own documented HTML behaviour is that key. Like the static checker it credits activation and text entry only, **never navigation**, and a test asserts that refusal plus the fact that `handled` and `platform` give **opposite** answers on the same Reka-shaped tree. |
| **Three seeded breaks, on component SOURCE and never on a test, each failing EXACTLY the predicted tests** | `sha256` of all three files recorded **before** the first seed; every restore the **inverse edit**, verified by `sha256sum -c` **OK (3 of 3)** after each; **`git checkout` was not used**. **(A)** `DzToolbar.vue` — delete the `case 'Home'` arm → gate **exit 1** naming the component and the key, `unbacked-declaration: 1 over the ceiling of 0`; spec **2 failed / 26 passed**, both predicted. **(B)** `DzListItem.vue` — narrow the guard to `event.key !== 'Enter'` so only Space is lost → gate **exit 1**; spec **3 failed / 6 passed**, all three predicted, and the six that stayed green (Enter, both refusals, Escape) are the proof the seed was surgical. **(C)** `DzTransfer.vue` — delete the `case 'End'` arm → gate **exit 1**; spec **6 failed / 23 passed**, every test that drives End and only those. **C is the one that matters most**: one deleted `case` takes down six tests, which is the evidence the tests assert an *effect* — where focus is — rather than the presence of a handler. A spec that only checked for a listener would have stayed green through all three. After the last restore the gate reads green again. |

What stands is **contention**: both failing runs were on a loaded machine, both clean runs on a quiet one. **Not new to this batch** — the same error is recorded at `docs/program-2026-09-04/reports/TASK-R5-O9-handoff.md:177`, and `vitest.config.ts`’s own comment records this suite already raising `testTimeout` 30 s → 60 s for a timeout that "timed out only in `yarn test:coverage`, i.e. only in the CI job that gates merges". The keys those play functions drive were checked against the keys this batch added **before** the run, so the expectation was specific: `DzOrderList` drives no printable character, so type-ahead cannot interfere; `DzCascader` drives arrows and Enter while this batch added only Home/End; `DzTour` drives Enter and Escape while this batch added the arrows; `DzToolbar`'s single `tab()` lands on the control that still carries `tabindex="0"`. |

The `chrome` delta is **+1** and is the user's own browser; it is **reported and not acted on**, because the batch that killed 37 `chrome.exe` by name may have closed the user's browser and the lane's engine is `chromium`, which is 0 both times. **No `taskkill`, no kill-by-name, no kill at all.** |
