## Residual batch — RESIDUAL-14 (RESIDUAL-13 §10 item 6), 2026-09-29

| | |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | RESIDUAL-13's own ranked item 6 — *"audit the other direction of the gate's one blind spot"*. Its §3.4 found that `DzTransfer`'s arrows **cited a real file for the wrong claim**, fixed that one component, and recorded that **366 backed citations had never been read against the action text they are supposed to support**. This batch reads them. No new task row; it closes register §19.6 and raises §20.1–§20.4. |
| **Commit observed** | **`4e4e46f`** — **396 → 402** dirty paths (**+6, all additions**: four anatomy files that were clean at entry, the rejected-citations ledger and the changeset — every one attributed in the report §8), **0 removed, 0 status letters changed**, measured by diffing the two sorted listings. The entry listing was snapshotted to the session scratchpad **outside** the repository first. Nothing reverted, stashed, checked out, cleaned, pulled, merged, rebased or committed. `yarn.lock` **untouched**. No `yarn install`, no baseline replacement, no screenshot. One probe directory (`packages/tooling/src/validators/__probe/`, two throwaway `tsx` measurements) was created and **removed before any gate ran**; `git status` confirms nothing under it survives. **No process killed and none needed killing** — no lane was run. |
| **Report** | [`./reports/RESIDUAL-14-keyboard-citation-audit-handoff.md`](./reports/RESIDUAL-14-keyboard-citation-audit-handoff.md) |
| **Outcome — the headline is a number, and it is worse than the gate said** | **A census, not a sample: all 404 rows adjudicated against all 220 distinct citation sites.** **352 citations genuinely back what their row says the key does (87.1 %). 52 name a real file for the wrong claim (12.9 %). 0 were undecidable.** Of the 52, **12 are rows that are themselves false** — the citation is wrong because there is nothing to cite. So *"404 of 404 backed"* was worth 352. **`maxUnbackedDeclarations` 0 → measured 9. `maxUndeterminedDeclarations` 0 → measured 7. NEITHER CEILING WAS RAISED and the gate is RED.** |
| **Why a census rather than a sample** | The 404 rows collapse to **220 distinct `file:line` citations in 90 files**, which is small enough to open every one. Two mechanical screens ran first to direct the reading — *is the cited line inside a comment?* and *does the cited line exist where the citation says?* — and both turned out to be findings in their own right. `U = 0` is therefore a claim with a basis: every site was read, including nine minified `reka-ui` dist lines quoted in the report. |
| **The `DzTransfer` root cause, generalised** | `ownerOf` resolved routes 1–4 with **two independent file-wide predicates that were never related to one another**: *does this FILE contain a keyboard handler* (`HANDLER_TOKEN` over the whole text) and *does this FILE name the key anywhere* (`keysNamedIn` over the whole text). Nothing required the handler that satisfied the first to be the one that named the key in the second, nor either to be bound to the node the row is about, nor the behaviour to resemble the row's `action`. The `platform` route was **already** scoped to the node the row is about — RESIDUAL-12 scoped it deliberately, to catch `DzChip` — and routes 1–4 were not. **That asymmetry is the blind spot.** |
| **Six mechanisms, measured** | **(1) A comment is evidence — 18 rows.** `templateNodesIn` anchored on `indexOf('<template>')`, so `i18n/useComponentMessages.ts` — a 130-line composable with **no markup** whose docblock `@example` contains `<template>` and a `<button>` — was the platform owner of six rows across `DzAnchor`, `DzBreadcrumb` and `DzSidebar`. `keysNamedIn` read `End:` out of a docblock sentence, `typeahead` out of two more (one of which *says the type-ahead never runs*), and `specAssertedKeys` harvested `platform: [' ']` and `handled: [' ']` from **inside comments**, the second from a sentence saying that assertion *"would be false"*. This is RESIDUAL-02/05's `filesMentioning` defect in a second artifact. **(2) First match wins — 25 rows**, in document or sorted-primitive order, with no relation to the row: `DzTimePicker`'s *"select the highlighted option"* was the popover **trigger**, `DzDataGrid`'s six grid rows were `RovingFocus` reached through a **row-selection checkbox**, `DzSplitButton`'s *"activate the primary action"* was a **menu** primitive. **(3) `modifiers` ignored — 5 rows.** The field has been parsed and validated since `anatomy-source.ts:403` and this validator keyed **only** on `binding.key`, so `Shift`+`PageUp` → *"move to the previous year"* was satisfied by `subtract({ months: 1 })`. **(4) `when` not applied to routes 1–4 — 2 rows**: `DzSearchInput`'s `Enter` for the **clear** control was the `Enter` that **submits**. **(5) A dynamic component credited with `<button>` activation whatever its `is=` can resolve to — 2 rows**: `DzTableCell`'s root is `is="header ? 'th' : 'td'"`. **(6) The closure itself built from prose — 6 rows**: the `<Dz…>` render-edge walk read docblock `@example` blocks, so five slot-based containers' closures were **the consumer's example markup**. |
| **The largest single false green** | **`DzDataGrid` publishes six `apg: 'grid'` cell-navigation rows and navigates no cells.** `role="grid"` at `DzDataGrid.vue:179`, `role="gridcell"` twice in `DzDataGridBody.vue`, and in the **whole family**: no arrow handling, no roving `tabindex`, no `reka-ui` import. Its only keyboard is the header's sort and the filter popover's `Escape`. The gate's own `what` field named the primitive it credited — `CheckboxIndicator`. Raised as **`D-RES14-1`**; a one-dimensional roving-focus group cannot implement a two-dimensional pattern. |
| **What was tightened — five structural rules, each proved both ways** | **`R1`** comments are not evidence: `stripCommentsForScan` blanks every comment **and preserves the line count**, its output *is* `SourceFile.source` so every downstream scan inherits it, `.vue` is handled in two regions (HTML comments everywhere, the JS scanner only inside `<script>`), and `templateNodesIn` anchors on a **top-level** `/^<template>/m`. It reuses RESIDUAL-05's own `stripComments` with a new `preserveLines` option — whose docblock already *claimed* line stability and did not have it. **`R2`** a declared modifier is part of the contract: routes 1–4 and 5 require the modifier's property in the **innermost brace block containing the citing line, including its opening line**, and the platform route cannot satisfy a modified row at all except `Shift`+`Tab`. **`R3`** `PLATFORM_ACTIVATION_TOKEN` **reads the dynamic component's expression**. **`R4`** `HANDLER_TOKEN` is case-insensitive **and boundary-free on the bare word** — `useDataGridHeader.ts` spells it `handleHeaderKeyDown` at all six occurrences, so `\bkeydown\b` failed on the capitals *and* on a leading `\b` that cannot hold inside an identifier, and **a missed owner produces a worse owner, not no owner**. **`R5`** a part-scoped row admits the handler routes only if the part's own **subtree** binds a handler (`subtreeOf`, a region test because a declared part often is a region — `DzDataGrid`'s `header` is a `<thead>` and the handler is on a `<th>` inside it). |
| **The proof, RESIDUAL-05's way** | **21 new cases**, each **reconstructing the old predicate inline, showing it accept something wrong, then showing the shipped predicate reject it** — a permanent version of seeding rather than a one-off. `indexOf('<template>')` finds the docblock template and a `<button>` after it / `templateNodesIn(strip(…))` is empty · `keysNamedIn` finds `PageUp` at the month arm / `readsModifiersAt(…, ['Shift'])` is `false` there · the old `is=` regex matches `'th' : 'td'` / the new one refuses it and still accepts `'button' : 'div'` and `computedTag` · `/\bkeydown\b/` and `/\bkeydown\b/i` both fail on `handleHeaderKeyDown` / the shipped token matches **and** every `DzDataGrid` header row in the live report now cites `useDataGridHeader`. All 21 pass. |
| **20 of the 52 were repaired outright** | Six rows moved off a docblock `<button>` onto `DzAnchor.vue:180`, `DzBreadcrumbItem.vue:64` and `DzSidebarItem.vue:120`; six moved off `optionsStateFocus.ts`'s retry handler onto Reka's real combobox primitives; `DzDataGrid`'s three sort rows moved from `Checkbox/CheckboxRoot.js` and `Select/utils.js` onto `useDataGridHeader.ts:110`; `DzTreeSelect`'s two moved off their own docblocks; the two `spec`-route rows moved off a comment onto the real `expectKeyboardContract` call; `DzSearchInput`'s clear row moved onto the clear `<button>`. **And 39 platform citations that pointed at the wrong file line now point at the node** — `templateNodesIn` had been printing a template-body offset as a file line, so `DzButton.vue:2` was an `import type` and the node was at 190. |
| **The residue, recorded as data and enforced** | The 22 remaining wrong citations are wrong for a reason **no structural rule reaches**: a real handler, correctly bound, doing something other than what the sentence says. A checker can prove a citation is code and not prose, that it reads the declared modifier, that an element can be the element claimed, and that the real owner is not skipped; it cannot prove that a correctly-bound handler does what a sentence describes — RESIDUAL-05 drew the same line. So `packages/tooling/src/validators/anatomy-keyboard-rejected-citations.json` carries **25 entries over 22 rows**, each with the evidence a reader must be able to check, and the validator refuses them. Three properties make it safe: a rejected citation is **skipped** and the walk continues (hence `keyLinesIn`, which records every line a key is named on, not just the first), so the file **can only lower `backed`**; it **ratchets like a ceiling** and says so — *deleting an entry to recover a count is the same act as raising a ceiling*; and it is keyed on the **row** exactly, `when` and `modifiers` included, because `DzSearchInput.vue:168` really is the `Enter` that submits and is not the `Enter` that clears. |

| **Gates** (every exit code read from a log **file** at an absolute path, never from a notice, never through a pipe) | **`yarn validate:all` `EXIT=1` — 52 `✓`, exactly ONE `✗` link: `anatomy-keyboard`.** Every other link green, including `anatomy-parts` (626 emissions, 0/0 undeclared, 0/0 unemitted), `ownership-manifest` (1338 entries fresh, 29/29, 41/41), `component-meta` (fresh, 144), `capability-matrix` (fresh), `quality-tiers`, `rtl`, `llms`, `token-references`, `story-dod`. **`yarn test` `EXIT=1` — 578 files, 11,408 passed, 3 skipped, 1 todo, and exactly TWO failures**, both in `anatomy-keyboard.spec.ts`: *"is at or under every checked-in ceiling"* and *"closes both ratchets at zero, which is what RESIDUAL-13 was for"*. **They are the same fact the gate reports, and they are left failing on purpose** — softening either assertion would be preserving a count. **`yarn regenerate:all` `EXIT=0`, 7 of 7** (`nav.json` artifact sha256 `edf58a56a711…`; step 7's totals unchanged at 133). `yarn typecheck` **0** · `yarn typecheck:tooling` **0** · `eslint` over the 8 changed files **0**, after **7 errors + 2 warnings fixed BY HAND — `--fix` was not used**. |
| **Pre-existing vs new** | **New: only the two red gates, and they are one fact reported twice.** `validate:all` 53 `✓`/0 `✗` → 52 `✓`/1 `✗`; `yarn test` 0 failures → 2. **Pre-existing and untouched**: the `README.md links to ../../DESIGN.md` warn. **`D-RES13-2` did not occur** — the single `yarn test` run reported two *named* failing tests with assertion messages, not a phantom exit, so there was nothing to re-run and nothing to quote twice. |
| **No lane was run, and that is argued rather than assumed** | Nothing in this batch renders differently: no `.variants.ts`, token, class, template or script changed in any component. The five edited component files are `*.anatomy.ts` **declarations**; the rest is tooling, a ledger, a changeset and the report. A browser or visual run would have qualified nothing. RESIDUAL-11 §9.3's `DzSelect` item stays untouched. **No `kill`, no `taskkill`, no matching by process name at any point.** |
| **State** | **The gate is RED and that is the correct end state.** It will stay red until `D-RES14-1` … `D-RES14-4` are answered, and the fastest honest route to green is (a) decide `DzDataGrid`, (b) six `expectKeyboardContract` calls for the `Tab`-shaped `undetermined` rows, (c) four lines for `DzCalendar`. Nothing is blocked on an agent. |
| **Owner decisions** | **`D-RES14-1`** 🔴 **RAISED** — `DzDataGrid` publishes six grid-navigation rows and navigates no cells. Three options costed: (a) implement 2-D cell navigation (a day, plus a changeset that changes a published tab order), (b) withdraw the six rows and keep `role="grid"`, (c) withdraw the rows **and** the grid roles. **No recommendation**: (b) leaves a grid announcing a pattern it does not implement, which is arguably worse than an untrue table, and (a) and (c) are both published-behaviour changes. **6 rows `unbacked` until answered.** · **`D-RES14-2`** 🟡 **RAISED** — `DzTransfer` declares APG listbox type-ahead that has never existed; backed until now by the pane's **search field**, which filters. (a) implement it on the pane, modelled on `DzOrderList.vue:454` (~20 lines + tests; also ask whether the two become one shared helper), or (b) withdraw the row, since APG lists type-ahead as recommended and the panes are searchable. · **`D-RES14-3`** 🟡 **RAISED** — `DzCalendar`'s two `Shift`+page-key year rows. (a) own a menu, (b) withdraw `ArrowDown` and re-state `Escape` as a contract the `spec` route can evidence, (c) add a way to declare a requirement **on a slotted subtree** — there is no column for that today. |

One new question worth a packet of its own: **which other gate credits a claim because a file mentions a token?** This defect has now been found in two artifacts, six months apart, by accident both times; `validate:adr-references`, `hardcoded-strings` and the `at-matrix` are the candidates and all three publish. |

## Residual batch — RESIDUAL-15 (`D-RES14-1` … `D-RES14-4`), 2026-09-29

| | |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | Return the deliberately-red keyboard gate to green **by making its claims true** — the four decisions RESIDUAL-14 raised plus the seven `undetermined` rows, with the rule that a ceiling may not be touched until every row is genuinely backed or legitimately withdrawn. No new task row; this batch closes register §20.1–§20.4 and §20.6. |
| **Commit observed** | **`4e4e46f`** — **402 → 416** dirty paths (**+14, all additions**, every one attributed in the report §7.3), **0 removed, 0 status letters changed**, measured by diffing the two sorted listings. ` M yarn.lock` untouched — no `yarn install` was run. |
| **Report** | [`./reports/RESIDUAL-15-keyboard-gate-green-handoff.md`](./reports/RESIDUAL-15-keyboard-gate-green-handoff.md) |
| **Outcome** | **`yarn validate:anatomy-keyboard` reads 401 of 401 backed, 0 unbacked, 0 undetermined, 0 reverse drift — and every one of the 16 rows was closed by code or by a withdrawal with evidence, not by a ceiling.** `validate:all` **exit 0, 53 `✓`, zero `✗`**; `yarn test` **exit 0, 578 files, 11,451 passed, 0 failed**. Both gates green again with the ceilings at `0` because the rows are true. |
| **`D-RES14-1` decided — `DzDataGrid`, and the component decided it** | RESIDUAL-14 gave no recommendation, so the component was read first. Five facts made **(a) implement** tractable without restructuring anything: the cells are the family's **own** (`DzDataGridBody.vue:113` renders `<td role="gridcell">` from a `v-for`; the only `<slot />` is *inside* a cell), the grid ARIA was **already complete**, the root renders a real `<table>` so `HTMLTableElement.rows`/`row.cells` is an exact order-true cell address with header and body carrying the **same** cell count, one `DZ_DATA_GRID_KEY` context already reaches both sub-parts, and the mechanisms exist here (`DzToolbar`'s roving `tabindex`, `useDzDirection()`, `DzCalendar`'s arrow pair). Withdrawal is the answer for a grid whose cells a consumer supplies; this is not one, and RESIDUAL-14 itself said (b) *"leaves a grid that announces a grid pattern to a screen reader and does not implement it, which is arguably worse than an untrue docs table"*. |
| **What `DzDataGrid` now does** | New `useDataGrid/useDataGridNavigation.ts`: arrows on both axes with the inline pair **mirrored in RTL** (the `rtl: 'mirrored'` rows and `rtl.keyboard: 'swap-horizontal'` finally read), `Home`/`End` per row, **two new declared `PageUp`/`PageDown` rows** (ten rows, clamped, declared in the same change so `maxUndeclaredHandlers` stays 0), **no wrapping** (APG `grid` moves and stops), and a roving `tabindex` over the body's cells. The header participates: a non-sortable `<th>` moves from no `tabindex` to `-1`, which adds **no** tab stop and makes it reachable by `ArrowUp`. **Net tab-order change: one stop added, none removed** — every sortable header keeps its own and a control inside a cell keeps its own tab stop *and* its own keys, because navigation acts only when `event.target === event.currentTarget`. |
| **The rejected alternative inside (a), recorded in the anatomy** | **The strict single-tab-stop grid** (one stop, Enter/F2 to enter a cell's widget) was rejected because it *removes*: the per-column header tab stops `DzDataGrid.contract.spec.ts:136`/`:158` and `DzDataGrid.spec.ts:146` assert, every consumer's `#cell` control from the tab order, and it adds a modal idiom nothing else here uses — in exchange for conformance with a part of the pattern **no declared row states**, since this anatomy declares no `Tab` row at all. (b) and (c) are recorded beside it. |
| **`D-RES14-3` / `D-RES14-2` — implemented** | **`DzCalendar`**: the two page arms read `event.shiftKey ? cur.subtract({ years: 1 }) : cur.subtract({ months: 1 })` and the mirror — four lines, as costed; `setFocused` already moves the visible month. **`DzTransfer`**: `onPaneKeydown` already enumerated the options and resolved the focused one, so type-ahead is a `default:` arm plus one local `typeAheadIndex` reading each **rendered** option's `textContent` (what a screen reader announces, and it keeps working when `#item` is filled), searching after the focused option and wrapping, with `ownsItsOwnCaret` guarding a field inside an option. The pane's search `<input>` is a **sibling** of the `role="listbox"`, not a descendant, so it never reaches type-ahead. |
| **The shared-helper question, answered "no" with a measurement** | RESIDUAL-14 asked whether `DzOrderList`'s and `DzTransfer`'s type-ahead should become one helper. `keysNamedIn` reads **both** `/typeahead/i` **and** `event.key.length === 1` as the `<character>` placeholder, so a shared helper would hand **every importer** credit for listbox type-ahead through routes 1–4 — exactly what `utilities/keyboardTargets.ts`'s docblock refuses. Kept local, recorded in the function's docblock. |
| **`D-RES14-4` — withdrawn, and the deciding evidence was elsewhere** | Not merely "the menu is slotted": **`DzDropdownMenu` already publishes and implements both rows** — `DzDropdownMenu.anatomy.ts:96` declares `Escape` → *"Close the menu and return focus to the trigger."*, the **identical sentence**, backed through `DismissableLayer`, and `:66` declares `ArrowDown` while `DzDropdownMenuTrigger.vue` wraps Reka's `DropdownMenuTrigger`. So `ArrowDown` *(trigger)* and `Escape` *(menu open)* are withdrawn — RESIDUAL-14 §5's own `DzTable` → `DzDataGrid` move. `Escape` was **not** re-stated for the `spec` route, because the assertion it needs is about a **slotted menu's behaviour**, which is option (c)'s missing column. **Third row, not on the work list:** `Enter` *(trigger)* read `backed` on a real `<button>` while claiming *"open the menu and focus its first item"* — a latent `W` by RESIDUAL-14's own rule. Re-described to *"Activate the disclosure; the menu composed into its slot is what opens."*, `apg` `menu-button` → `button`. **No markup changed.** |
| **RESIDUAL-14's "mechanical" fix for the `Tab` rows does not work, and that is the finding** | §6 scheduled *"one `expectKeyboardContract` call each asserting `Tab` in its `platform` list… scheduled work, not judgement"*. **Measured, that call throws**: `PLATFORM_OWNERS` has no `Tab` entry and says why in its own docblock — *"activation and text entry only, **never navigation**"*. **The refusal is correct and was kept**: `Tab` is the document's focus order, not an element's behaviour. Worse, a key list cannot tell the five rows apart, because they claim **opposite** shapes — `DzCheckboxGroup`/`DzStepper`/`DzInfiniteScroll` say *each* node is its own tab stop, `DzRadioGroup`/`DzToolbar` say the set is *one* — and RESIDUAL-14 §2.2.4 caught exactly that inversion published. |
| **So the resolution was widened, never a ceiling** | `expectKeyboardContract` gains **`tabStops: { of, expect }`** (`@dzup-ui/testing: minor`): it names the shape, counts how many matched nodes are in the tab order, and **drives the key** — a cancelable `Tab` keydown the component must not have consumed. It refuses a set of fewer than two nodes, because a claim about order over one node is not a claim about anything. `specAssertedKeys` credits **`Tab` and only `Tab`**, anchored on the option's opening brace so prose grants nothing. This is precisely what the ceilings file's own comment says makes `undetermined` fall: *"widening the resolution shows up as THIS number falling rather than as `unbacked` rising"*. |
| **A measurement that changed the implementation** | `DzRadioGroup` first read *"0 of 2 matched node(s) are in the tab order"* — a **correct** implementation reported broken. Reka builds "one tab stop" on the **root** (`RovingFocusGroup.js`: `tabindex: isTabbingBackOut \|\| focusableItemsCount === 0 ? -1 : 0`; `RovingFocusItem.js`: `isCurrentTabStop ? 0 : -1`), so at rest the shape is `radiogroup=0 \| radio=-1 \| radio=-1`. `querySelectorAll` searches descendants only, so the root is now counted when it matches the selector — as the `platform` check already does. Its spec also records that the **synchronous** DOM reads `radiogroup=-1` (items register in their own `onMounted`) and awaits two ticks. |
| **Six `expectKeyboardContract` sites added, five carrying `tabStops`** | `DzCheckboxGroup.spec.ts:175` (`each`) · `DzRadioGroup.spec.ts:166` (`one`, over the radiogroup+radio union) · `DzStepper.spec.ts:268` (`each`, new import, `modelValue: 2` so all three steps are reachable) · `DzToolbar.spec.ts:338` (`one`) · `DzInfiniteScroll.spec.ts:172` (`each`, plus a loop asserting the feed consumes none of the APG `feed` keys). `undetermined` **7 → 0**, not 7 → 1: the sixth of RESIDUAL-14's six was `DzSplitButton` `Escape`, **withdrawn** rather than evidenced, and the seventh was `DzTransfer` `<character>`, **implemented**. |
| **The ledger — narrowed, never deleted** | Still **25 entries**. `DzTransfer` `<character>` was `DzTransfer.vue:*` — every line — which after implementation would have refused the **true** owner too. **Narrowed** to the two lines the panes' search `<input>`s sit on (`:414`, `:530`), which still do not back the row; the entry's own `why` records both halves. Route `own` is asked before `platform`, so it is now inert — which is the point: a guard against the citation coming back, not a subtraction. **`DzDataGrid`'s six `reka-ui/dist` entries were left in place** although the rule would now permit removal: they remain true, they cost nothing, and an inert entry is a ratchet. |
| **Proof — seeded breaks on component source, 1/2/2 exactly as predicted** | **A** `useDataGridNavigation.ts`, direction mirror removed → **1 of 53**: *mirrors the inline pair in a RTL document, which is what rtl: mirrored declares*. **B** `DzCalendar.vue`, `event.shiftKey ?` dropped from both page arms → **2 of 25**: *shift+PageUp moves focus back one year and emits panelChange* · *shift+PageDown moves focus forward one year, and the unmodified key still moves a month*. **C** `DzTransfer.vue`, `startsWith` → `includes` → **2 of 34**: *matches case-insensitively and skips a disabled option even when it is the only match* · *reads the label a consumer slotted, not the item, which is what a screen reader announces*. Each restored by byte copy and **verified with `sha256sum -c` → `OK`, exit 0** (the three sums are in report §6). **Never `git checkout`, never `git restore`, no scripted bulk replacement on source.** |
| **Gates** (every exit code read from a log **file** at an absolute path, never through a pipe) | **`yarn validate:all` `EXIT=0` — 53 `✓`, ZERO `✗`.** `anatomy-keyboard` reads *"401 declared bindings across 85 components; 401 backed, 0/0 unbacked, 0/0 undetermined"* and *"reverse drift: 0/0"*. `anatomy-parts` **626 emissions, 0/0, 0/0**; `ownership-manifest` **1341 entries fresh, 29/29 unclassified, 41/41 without anatomy**; `component-meta`, `capability-matrix`, `quality-tiers`, `llms`, `token-references`, `rtl`, `story-dod` all green. **`yarn test` `EXIT=0` — 578 files, 11,451 passed, 3 skipped, 1 todo, ZERO failed** (was 11,408 passed / 2 failed). `yarn regenerate:all` **7 of 7 exit 0** (run twice; the second after a spec edit that landed during the first `validate:all`). `yarn typecheck` **0**, `yarn typecheck:tooling` **0**, `eslint` over the 22 changed files **0** — three findings fixed **by hand**, never with `--fix`. |

| **Two scanner defects found before any verdict was trusted** | A regex comment-stripper read `accept: 'image/*'` (`ssr-smoke.spec.ts:527`) as an open block comment and blanked **378 lines** — precisely what `stripComments`'s own docblock warns about, so the census was re-run against that hand-scanned function. And paren-matching a test block must skip **regex literals**: `/data-state="([^"]*)"/g` reads as a string that swallows the `(`, and two blocks ran to EOF (731→852, 813→1206), handing **every overlay** the DzStepper test's teleport assertions and turning 9 wrong verdicts into 10 right-looking ones. |
| **Mechanism 1 — `portal-hydration` was `ssr-sample` under another name** | Both cells called the **same predicate over the same directory**, so the kind had no term for a teleport and none for hydration: any component loaded by any file under `packages/core/tests/ssr/` got it. Of 19 citations, **3** take the portal branch (`DzSidebar`, `DzTour`, `DzCommandPalette`), **8** render the component *closed* so the `<Teleport>` stays behind a false `v-if` — the exact defect RESIDUAL-10 fixed in two tests and left in eight — **8** have no teleport in the test at all, and **0 hydrate anything**. `form-controls-ssr.spec.ts` says so in its own header: *"What this does not do is drive a hydration mismatch."* |
| **Mechanism 2 — 27 `state-stories` cells were `pass` because nobody asked. THE `pass` FINDING.** | `storyCheck` asked one question — *is this component absent from the check's failing set?* — and a component the check does not **apply** to is absent from it too. `states` is the check with a non-trivial `applies` clause (`applicable=56` of 170 story files). So 27 cells read **`pass`** while citing a story file with no `States` story in it: 25 Tier B and 2 Tier C, named in the report §3.2(a). `DodCheckResult` carried `applicable` as a **count**, so its one consumer could not tell *passed* from *never asked*. |
| **A measurement that corrected the fix itself** | The first tightening asked applicability first and moved **36** cells — **9 wrongly**. `DzInput`, `DzTextarea`, `DzNumberInput`, `DzPasswordInput`, `DzSearchInput`, `DzListbox`, `DzMention`, `DzAnchor` and `DzCascader` export a real `States` story; `states` never asks them because their state props are **inherited** from `BaseFormControlProps` while `story-dod.ts` reads the component's own `.types.ts` (narrowed deliberately by N1-O1 defect D6). The shipped resolution asks *does the story satisfy the check?* **first**, and applicability only to explain a component that does not — so `passingFiles` was added beside `applicableFiles`, and `story-dod.spec.ts` asserts the two disagree **in both directions**. |
| **What was tightened** | New `packages/tooling/src/quality/spec-capability-refs.ts`: it **wraps** `filesLoading` rather than replacing it (loading stays the floor a comment cannot reach) and adds one requirement — a **live** test block must name the component *and* exercise the capability. `axe` → an `axe(…)`/`toHaveNoViolations` call; `ssr-sample` → `renderToString` or a file-level helper reaching it; `portal-hydration` → that **and** the portal branch taken (anchor pair, or `open: true`). `storyCheck` gains four arms. |
| **Proof, both directions, per rule** | 24 new cases in two new spec files + 4 in `story-dod.spec.ts`; **28 tests, all pass**. Each reconstructs the old predicate inline: the old rule credits `ssrRender(DzDialog, { open: false })` for `portal-hydration` → the new one rejects it **and still credits it for `ssr-sample`**, so the rejection is not a blanket one · the old rule credits the dynamic import inside `it.skip` → the new one rejects it, and crediting returns when `it.skip(` becomes `it(` · the old rule credits `cards.a11y.spec.ts`'s non-axe block → the new one rejects it and credits the axe block · and the RESIDUAL-05 floor is re-asserted (a *"tested elsewhere"* note grants nothing; a docblock quoting `await axe(container)` is not an axe run). A fourth `axe` case pins the **inline-template root** shape, which 23 of the 61 `axe` citations take. |
| **Capability movement — and `pass` fell** | **`pass` 585 → 558 (−27)** · `present` **623 → 608 (−15)** · `unrun` **385 → 400 (+15)** · `excepted` **47 → 74 (+27)** · `stale` **22 → 22** · `fail` **0 → 0**. All 27 `pass` losses are `state-stories`, now `excepted` with a derived reason and a citation of the component's `.types.ts` instead of its story file. All 15 `present` losses are `portal-hydration` → `unrun`. One cell lost an artifact without changing state (`DzAccordion` `ssr-sample` 3 → 2, the `it.skip`). **Nothing else moved**: the tightened `axe` predicate accepted all 61 of its citations. |
| **The gate is GREEN, and that is finding 3 (`D-RES16-3`)** | `yarn validate:capability-matrix` **exit 0** — `✓ fresh, and no Tier D cell is unexplained` · `unrun 400 (baseline 400)`, drift **zero** · `22 stale — ceiling 22` · browser-degradation **0 of 2,112**. A change that removed 27 false `pass` cells and 15 false `present` cells **fails nothing**, because **no ratchet in this repository holds `pass` or `present` at all**. That is the same asymmetry RESIDUAL-02, RESIDUAL-14 and this packet have each paid for: *"the totals did not move, so no gate fired."* |

It is the price of the correction: those eight cells had an artifact that did not evidence the claim, and now they honestly have none. |

The diff is exactly one line. ` M yarn.lock` untouched — no `yarn install`, and `npx` was never used. |

The harness hydrates **4** components with **0 bytes rewritten**, re-credits 4 cells (2 of them new), **takes 1 away** (`DzCommandPalette`), and leaves **20** `unrun` with a reason that is itself asserted. |
| **Item 1 — gate 8, `evidence-ratchet`** | Three clauses. **(a)** per kind, per state, **both directions** — the message names the kind, the state, the direction and the delta (`` `state-stories` / pass ROSE 60 → 87 (+27) ``); per **kind** because a bare 558 is equally satisfied by trading 27 real passes in one column for 27 invented ones in another, and that swap is pinned by a test. **(b)** the **kind set** is pinned — all **23** recorded, zeros included, so a new column arriving with credit already in it is an error and so is a column vanishing; "zero credit" is deliberately distinguished from "the row is gone". **(c)** `totals` must equal the sum of `kinds`, so the quoted headline cannot drift from its own table. |
| **Baseline seeded, measured not copied** | `pass` **558** · `present` **608** over **1,662** cells and **23** kinds, derived from the artifact **by script** — and **never** the pre-audit 585/623, because seeding a ratchet at numbers an audit has just falsified writes the inflation into the record permanently. |
| **A legitimate move is ONE change, and honesty was kept cheap** | One edit to the kind's entry in `capability-matrix-ceilings.json` in the same change, plus one line in its `//moves` log — the existing `staleCells.ceiling` handshake, pointed both ways. The gate prints the **literal replacement** in the message *and* the **whole corrected block** ready to paste, and a test **parses that printed block and re-runs the gate against it**, so the offered remedy is asserted rather than promised. There is deliberately **no `--write-baseline` flag**: a ratchet an agent can discharge with a command is not a ratchet. RESIDUAL-16 rightly *lowered* `pass` by 27 and future audits will too — that must cost one paste, not an afternoon. |
| **Item 1 proved BOTH ways, end to end** | **RISE:** `storyCheck` reverted to RESIDUAL-16's exact pre-audit inversion → exit **1**, `` `state-stories` / pass ROSE 60 → 87 (+27) `` — **the RESIDUAL-16 change replayed, and it now fails**. **FALL:** `DzCalendar.stories.ts`'s `export const Accessibility` renamed → exit **1**, `` `a11y-narrative` / pass FELL 22 → 21 (-1) `` — the direction a ceiling would have let through for free. A **negative control** on `DzButton` (Tier A, no such cell) correctly stayed **green at exit 0**: the gate did not fire on a seed that changed no evidence. Every seed byte-copied first and restored, `sha256sum -c` `OK`. **+12 unit cases** (16 → 28). |

| **Item 1 — one mechanism, not a class of sites** | All 58 come from **one third-party function reached from one call site**. `@formkit/auto-animate`'s cold poll (`index.mjs:159`) is two-stage: an outer `setTimeout` **whose id is stored nowhere**, which later installs a 2 s `setInterval`. `controller.destroy()` can cancel **neither** — the timeout because no handle exists, the interval because `destroy()` walks `parent.children`, which no longer holds a child detached earlier (`cleanUp()` at `index.mjs:499` clears that node's coords, siblings, animations and IntersectionObserver but **not** its interval). `poll()` is called only from `autoAnimate()`, over the parent **plus its direct children**, so **58 is the `/animations` bento's own arithmetic**: the grid plus one per catalogue card. `lowPriority()` (`index.mjs:168`) ends at `requestAnimationFrame` because jsdom has no `requestIdleCallback`. **Invisible in a browser** — nothing tears the page down under it — and a per-visit leak there all the same. |
| **Item 1 — what was fixed, in source** | **New `apps/landing/src/motion/autoAnimate.ts`** owns both stages at the only point that sees them: for the duration of the single **synchronous** `autoAnimate()` call it wraps `globalThis.setTimeout` to record every stage-1 id, and runs each recorded callback with `globalThis.setInterval` wrapped to record the stage-2 id it installs. `destroy()` delegates to the library's own and then cancels both sets. Both wrappers restore **the value they replaced**, in a `finally`, so an outer instrumentation keeps its place in the chain. The motion barrel now exports `autoAnimate` / `vAutoAnimate` / `useAutoAnimate` from there **instead of from `@formkit/auto-animate/vue`**, `AutoAnimateListDemo.vue` imports through the barrel, and the `auto-animate-list` registry item was regenerated so it **bundles the wrapper** and a copied-out snippet inherits the fix. Two further adapter defects fixed on the way: the library's `watchEffect` never destroys the controller for a host it just left, and its returned ref is typed `Ref<T>` when it is `undefined` until mount. |
| **The test-local workaround was DELETED, which is the point** | `AnimationsPage.v2.spec.ts` had already diagnosed this defect — its header quotes `poll()` and records **127** unhandled errors — and patched it **inside that one file**, wrapping the global scheduler, filtering on whether the stack contained `auto-animate`, sweeping after every test. **32 lines gone.** `cleanup()` is now the whole teardown. Its "guard on the guard" is replaced by one that pins what rots quietly: that the bento renders its AutoAnimate branch **and** that the barrel's `vAutoAnimate` is the wrapper and **not** npm's — because anyone "simplifying" the barrel back to the one-liner brings 127 errors back while every test in that file still passes. |
| **Item 1 — the contract** | `apps/landing/src/motion/autoAnimate.spec.ts`, **8 tests**, asserting on **time** rather than markup. Each teardown test is a **pair**: first that the poll machinery is genuinely armed in the fixture (advance the clock while mounted → frames requested), then that teardown silences it (advance after unmount → not one frame) — without the first half the second would pass on a reduced-motion or empty fixture. Plus the **upstream tripwire**: *the unwrapped library still schedules a frame after its own `destroy()`*. When that fails, `poll()` has been fixed upstream and the wrapper can be deleted; the failure message says so. |

| **Criterion** | The cell's own sentence in `packages/contracts/src/quality-tiers.ts` — *"Contract Spec v1 props/events/slots/ARIA"* and *"render and behaviour units"*. A surface is **owed** only when the component's own `.types.ts` or template declares it, and **touched** when the spec's live code (comments stripped, skipped blocks blanked) has the term. **A surface-touched predicate, not a conformance proof**: one touch per surface, it cannot tell an assertion from its negation (pinned by a test), and applicability is conservative so it can under-ask but not over-ask. |
| **Verdicts** | `contract-spec` **62 G / 80 W** (A 22 · B 37 · C 20 · D 1) — 41 miss `events`, 44 `slots`, 34 `aria`, 36 miss more than one. `unit-spec` **127 G / 14 W**, all `no-behaviour`. `DzCheckbox.contract.spec.ts` has nine tests and none mentions `change`; `DzInput.spec.ts` has 23 and never types into the input. **50 of the 80 are covered when the pair of sidecars is read together; 30 are covered by neither file.** |

## How to fill a row

- **Status** — one of `[ ]` `[~]` `[x]` `[x] found-done` `[!]`.
- **Commit observed** — the commit the task's numbers are bound to, plus the
  dirty-path count if the worktree was not clean. A number without a commit is
  not evidence.
- **Report** — relative link to `./reports/<TASK-ID>-handoff.md`.
- **Ratchets / owner decisions** — `old → new` for every ratchet the task
  moved, and a numbered line per decision raised, with options and a
  recommendation.
- **done_check outcome** — record it explicitly as `n of m at <commit>`, and if
  a check was defective, say which one and what you decided from instead.
